# Obligations register

> ⚠️ **Retained render — not the authored origin.** Under `D-REGULATORY-ARCHITECTURE-TWO-PLANE` the canonical authored origin of the bank-obligation register is **`Regulations/_obligations.seed.json`**, folded into `ObligationAdopted` events (Plane B) by `bun run backfill:obligations`. **Edit the seed, not this file** — regenerate via `bun run scripts/generate-obligations-seed.ts`. `recon:obligations-seed-parity` asserts this render has not drifted from the seed. (Existing readers still consume this markdown during the reader-migration follow-up.)

**Curator:** Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer) · **Governance:** Zara (Chief Compliance Officer) · **Date:** 2026-06-07 · **Version:** 1.44

> v1.44 — **LCR obligation rows confirmed/extended on authoring of the BA 110 (LCR) instrument analysis (`ORG-PR-06`, `ORG-PR-RETURNS-003`).** Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)), with domain co-owners Eitan (Treasurer, governance) — liquidity / LCR / HQLA methodology + BA 110 LCR-figure ownership, and Bea (Accounting & financial reporting engineer, engineering — reports to Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance)) — BA 110 form-line compilation. Produced alongside the newly-POPULATED `Regulations/SARB-PA/ba-returns/ba-110.md` (the analysis the BA 110 STUB row in `Regulations/_index.md` had been awaiting; `_index.md` POPULATED count 9 → 10). **Source verified against the in-corpus primary text:** D5/2025 §2.1.4 verbatim (`Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d5-2025-structured.json`, `/chapters[0]/sections[1]/subsections[0]/subsections[3]`: *"Complete form BA 110, attached to this Directive as Annexure 3A, in accordance with the requirements specified in Annexure 3B … read with the relevant requirements specified in the Regulations."*); **Regulation 26** verbatim (`Regulations/SARB-PA/source-docs/rrb-structured.json`, `/chapters[1]/sections[8]`, `sectionNumber: "Regulation 26"` — *"Liquidity risk … for completion of monthly return concerning liquidity risk"*; Reg 26(2)(a)–(e) purpose; Reg 26(3) GL-reconciling audit trail); BCBS LCR Pillar-1 spine (`Regulations/BCBS/lcr-liquidity-coverage.md`, provisions `urn:reg:bcbs:lcr:20.5/30.43/40.1/40.77`). **`ORG-PR-06` (LCR maintenance):** Citation cell **resolved** — legacy `"BCBS D295 / BA 325"` replaced with the canonical chain **BCBS LCR (`urn:reg:bcbs:lcr:20.5`, d295 lineage) → Reg 26 → D5/2025 §2.1.4 form BA 110**. The legacy **BA 325** form number was the superseded GG 35950 (12 December 2012) schedule number; the canonical current number is **BA 110** per D5/2025 §2.1.4 (`D-SARB-RETURN-SCHEDULE-CANONICAL-D5-2025`). The opaque `[TBD]` URN is **left unchanged** — it is a replay-sensitive identity field, not an interpretive label (same posture as the v1.42 BA 340 URN). **`ORG-PR-RETURNS-003` (BA 110 = LCR return):** confirmed correct — annotation already reads BA 110 = LCR liquidity return (D5/2025 §2.1.4 + Reg 26), fully resolved at v1.36; no change. *(The BA 110 returns row is `ORG-PR-RETURNS-003`; the brief's reference to `ORG-PR-RETURNS-002` is a mis-reference — `ORG-PR-RETURNS-002` is the BA 100 capital-adequacy return.)* **Honesty notes (Principle 2):** the **Annexure 3B BA 110 line-completion schedule** and the **exact submission cadence/due-date** are NOT in the in-corpus extract (only the D5/2025 §2.1.4 routing instruction is) — kept `[citation: TBC]` in `ba-110.md`, flagged as the BA 110 line-item-generator substrate gap (Bea + Imani at the licence gate); the **significant-currency materiality threshold** for the Reg 26(2)(d) FX-LCR section is likewise counsel-gated. The Reg 26 verbatim text still embeds the GG-era **"Form BA 300"** label internally — noted in `ba-110.md` provenance; the canonical number is BA 110. **No obligation rows added, removed, or renumbered (count stays 417); no key field (id/fulfilmentPolicy/owner/reviewStatus) changed — seed parity holds without regeneration; only the `ORG-PR-06` Citation cell, this note, and the version line are touched.** Authority: standing register-curator mandate (Mira under Zara) + CEO decision `D-ROADMAP-WS-C-RECONCILE` (approved 2026-06-07). Brief: `brief:mira:ba-110-lcr-instrument-analysis:2026-06-07`. (PR by Mira.)

> v1.43 — **IRRBB obligation rows confirmed/extended on authoring of the BA 330 (IRRBB) instrument analysis (`ORG-PR-RETURNS-014`, `ORG-PR-34`).** Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)), with domain co-owners Eitan (Treasurer, governance) — ALM/IRRBB/ALCO, Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — IRRBB measurement methodology, and Bea (Accounting & financial reporting engineer, engineering — reports to Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance)) — BA 330 compilation. Produced alongside the newly-POPULATED `Regulations/SARB-PA/ba-returns/ba-330.md` (the genuinely-new IRRBB analysis the renamed `_index.md` STUB row had been awaiting). **Source verified against the public primary text** (SARB PA Directive 2 of 2023 PDF, ref. `15/8/2/3`, signed Fundi Tshazibana, CEO of the PA, 22/02/2023, and BCBS d368, both text-layer extracted 2026-06-07 via `pdftotext`). **`ORG-PR-RETURNS-014` (BA 330 = IRRBB):** confirmed correct — no change. The analysis resolves the substantive content from D2/2023 verbatim: §2.1.1 repricing-gap construction; lines 45–66 ΔNII (parallel + adverse-correlated shocks, 12-month forward horizon, % of Tier 1 per BA 700 line 77); lines 67–93 ΔEVE (NPV-discount of the static gap, Jibar swap base curve per **Reg 39(4)**); the six prescribed shock scenarios per **Reg 30(5)(a)(iii)(H)(i)**; lines 94–99 the supervisory outlier test (max-loss ÷ Tier 1, the SARB transposition of **BCBS d368 Principle 12** — worst-case ΔEVE > 15% of Tier 1 = outlier); line 103 material foreign-currency reporting (≥ 5% of total assets/liabilities). **`ORG-PR-34` (PC 15/2024 CSRBB field testing):** confirmed; the `[citation: TBC]` for the BCBS IRRBB CSRBB clause references is **partially anchored but kept TBC** — the BCBS d368 CSRBB *definition* is now established (**d368 §2 para 10**: *"CSRBB refers to any kind of asset/liability spread risk of credit-risky instruments that is not explained by IRRBB and by the expected credit/jump to default risk"*), recorded in `ba-330.md`; but the **specific d368 clause numbers PC 15/2024 itself intends** remain genuinely counsel-gated (PC 15/2024 references the proposed CSRBB Directive framework without citing d368 clause numbers, and the standalone SARB CSRBB Directive is not finalised) — Imani (Legal-as-code engineer) + external counsel to resolve at the licence gate. An honest TBC is recorded rather than a guessed citation (Principle 2). **No obligation rows added, removed, or renumbered (count stays 417); no key field (id/fulfilmentPolicy/owner/reviewStatus) changed — seed parity holds without regeneration; the verbatim §2.1.x / D2-2023 quotes are unchanged; only this note + the version line are touched.** Authority: standing register-curator mandate (Mira under Zara) + CEO decision `D-ROADMAP-WS-C-RECONCILE` (approved 2026-06-07). Brief: `brief:mira:ba-330-irrbb-instrument-analysis:2026-06-07`. (PR by Mira.)

> v1.41 — **SARB return-form schedule ruled canonical: D5/2025 supersedes GG 35950 (`D-SARB-RETURN-SCHEDULE-CANONICAL-D5-2025`).** Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)), with engineering co-owner Bea (Accounting & financial reporting engineer, engineering — reports to Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance)). Marc (CEO) ruled in-session (2026-06-07) that the **SARB Prudential Authority Directive 5 of 2025 (D5/2025)** return-form schedule is **canonical** for BA-return form numbering, superseding the Regulations Relating to Banks (GG 35950, 12 December 2012) form schedule used by the v1.40 cross-check. **Decision `D-SARB-RETURN-SCHEDULE-CANONICAL-D5-2025`** (CEO, `marc@tgv.co.za`, `scrooge:session-delegation`, category governance; supersedes `D-BA-RETURN-FORM-NUMBERING-RECON`; cites GG 35950 superseded + D2/2023). **Canonical D5/2025 schedule (verbatim source: `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d5-2025-structured.json`, §2.1.x; the `ORG-PR-RETURNS-001..022` rows below carry the full per-form coverage):** BA 100 = capital adequacy (§2.1.3); BA 110 = LCR (§2.1.4); BA 120 = NSFR (§2.1.5); BA 200 = credit risk (§2.1.8); BA 210 = counterparty credit risk / SA-CCR (§2.1.9); BA 300 = operational risk (§2.1.11); BA 310 = market risk standardised (§2.1.12); BA 320 = alternative market risk (§2.1.13); BA 325 = FRTB market risk (§2.1.14); BA 330 = completed per D2/2023 (IRRBB — see below) (§2.1.15); BA 340 (§2.1.16); BA 400 = leverage ratio (§2.1.18); BA 600 = balance sheet (§2.1.21); BA 610 = income statement (§2.1.22); BA 700 = PVA + additional leverage disclosure (§2.1.23). **BA-330/IRRBB verdict (holds — `#1064`/`#1065` NOT unwound):** the verbatim D5/2025 §2.1.15 text directs that **form BA 330 is completed "in accordance with the requirements specified in Directive 2 of 2023"**, and **D2/2023 is "the Directive for the completion of the form BA 330"** under **Reg 30 (IRRBB)** — independently confirmed against the public SARB source (the 2026 proposed directive "revised requirements related to interest rate risk in the banking book", Annexure 1, governs BA 330; the BA 330 completion instructions were moved out of Reg 30(10) into a Directive). Therefore **BA 330 = IRRBB** in D5/2025, consistent with the v1.39 material finding and the v1.40 re-attribution sweep. The reporting code layer (`prototype/platform/reporting/*`, `prototype/platform/returns/*`) is re-mapped from GG-35950 to D5/2025 numbering in the same PR (balance sheet → BA 600, income statement → BA 610, market risk → BA 310, LCR → BA 110, NSFR → BA 120, operational risk → BA 300, leverage → BA 400, capital adequacy → BA 100; off-balance-sheet has no standalone D5/2025 return and is parked under a descriptive module name pending its credit-risk/CCR/balance-sheet line allocation). **Flagged residual annotation discrepancy (NOT corrected here — register-cell territory of `#1065`):** the `ORG-PR-RETURNS-014` Citation/requirement cells still read "BA 330 is the large exposures return" / "BA 340 is the IRRBB return" — interpretive labels the v1.39 note already declared WRONG. They are left unchanged in this code-focused pass to avoid colliding with the `#1065` register branch; a follow-up should reconcile the `ORG-PR-RETURNS-014`/`-015` annotation cells to BA 330 = IRRBB and re-home the large-exposures and equity-risk subjects per the verbatim D5 schedule. No obligation rows added, removed, or renumbered in v1.41; the verbatim §2.1.x quotes are unchanged; only this note + the `Regulations/_index.md` reference table + the reporting code layer are touched. Authority: standing register-curator mandate (Mira under Zara) + CEO decision `D-SARB-RETURN-SCHEDULE-CANONICAL-D5-2025`. Brief: `brief:mira:re-map-reporting-regulatory-form-numbers-to-the-:2026-06-07`. (PR by Mira; force-push of #1066, stacked on #1065.)

> v1.40 — **BA-330 re-attribution sweep — IRRBB vs large-exposures vs FX-NOP (ORG-PR-09).** Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) completes the repo-wide re-attribution of form **BA 330** following the v1.39 form-number finding, extending the correction from the register cells into the code and the remaining large-exposures register row. **Verified mapping (primary source — Regulations relating to Banks, GG 35950 of 12 December 2012, form schedule, cross-checked against the SARB PA *Proposed Directive — Returns to be submitted to the PA*, 2024):** **BA 320 = Market risk** (market-risk capital/RWA); **BA 325 = Daily return: selected risk exposure from trading and treasury activities** — carries the FX **effective net open position** attestation under **regulation 29(3)**; **BA 330 = Interest-rate risk: banking book (IRRBB)** (completed per D2/2023, reg 30); **BA 340 = Equity risk in the banking book**; **BA 350 = Derivative instruments**. The **FX net open position / market-risk return is BA 320 (with the daily NOP attestation on BA 325 / reg 29(3)), NOT BA 350 and NOT BA 330**; the **large-exposures regime (Reg 24(6)–(8) + D3/2022) reports via the BA 200-series credit-risk family**. **Register change (this version):** `ORG-PR-09` — removes the residual "/ BA 330" form attribution from the BCBS-Large-Exposures row (large exposures is not a BA 330 obligation). No other rows added, removed, or renumbered. **Code corrected in the same PR (Plane A reference attributions, not register cells):** `prototype/platform/regulatory/basel-adoption.ts` (LEX comment: "Reg 38 / BA 330" → Reg 24(6)–(8) + D3/2022 / BA 200-series); `prototype/platform/semantic/entries.ts` (large-exposures entries: form "BA 330" → BA 200-series / Reg 24 + D3/2022); `prototype/platform/projections/markets/limit-utilisation.ts` + `prototype/platform/event-store/event-types/trading.ts` (FX-NOP comments: "BA 330" → BA 320 market risk / BA 325 reg 29(3) NOP). The IRRBB exemplar `calc-model-definitions.ts` ("BA 330 IRRBB return") was already correct and left unchanged. Authority: standing register-curator mandate (Mira under Zara) + CEO decision **`D-BA-330-REATTRIBUTION-IRRBB`** (approved 2026-06-07), citing `D-ROADMAP-WS-C-RECONCILE`, the BCBS RCAP April 2023 report, and SARB D2/2023. Brief: `brief:mira:ba-330-re-attribution-sweep-irrbb-vs-large-expos:2026-06-07`. (PR by Mira; stacked on #1064.)

> v1.39 — **Large-exposures (LEX) citation resolution + BA-330 form-number correction (ORG-PR-40, ORG-PR-RETURNS-014).** Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) resolves the **verifiable large-exposures regulation references** against the public source — the **BCBS RCAP "Assessment of Basel large exposures regulations — South Africa" (April 2023, bis.org/bcbs/publ/d549.pdf)** and **SARB PA Directive 2 of 2023** (the BA-330 form-completion directive) — produced alongside the newly-populated `Regulations/SARB-PA/ba-returns/ba-330.md`. **Citation-discipline posture (Principle 2):** only references the public source unambiguously supports were resolved; precise Reg-24 sub-sub-section indices beyond those the RCAP verifies, the D3/2022 covering-directive body clauses, and the exact large-exposures return form number + cadence remain **counsel-gated** (Imani (Legal-as-code engineer) + external counsel at the licence gate). No citation was invented. **Resolved (verified):** `ORG-PR-40` — **CORRECTS the prior incorrect "Reg 28"** enabling-regulation attribution to **Reg 24(6)–(8)** read with Reg 23(8)/23(9)/23(18) (RCAP Annex 2 Table A.1), adds Banks Act s.90 enabling section + GG 46159 of 31 March 2022 (eff. 1 Apr 2022) + the RCAP sub-section map (definition = 24(7)(a)+24(6)(f); exemptions = 24(8)(a); CRM providers = 24(6)(d)(v); look-through = 24(6)(c)(iii)); the BCBS LEX anchors confirmed as para 14 (10% definition) + para 10.8 (25% cap) + FAQ Sept 2016. **Material finding (corrected):** `ORG-PR-RETURNS-014` — the prior annotation "**BA 330 is the large exposures return**" is **WRONG**; form **BA 330 is the IRRBB repricing-gap return** (Reg 30 / D2/2023, gazetted 23 Dec 2022, eff. 1 Jan 2023 — the same directive D5/2025 §2.1.15 references), and the LEX regime is reported within the credit-risk return family (BA 200-series); the prior "Reg 25" LEX attribution is also wrong (LEX = Reg 24(6)–(8)). The verbatim D5/2025 §2.1.15 quote is preserved unchanged (it is internally consistent with BA 330 = IRRBB); only the incorrect interpretive annotations were corrected. **Left counsel-gated (deliberate, honest outcome — not a failure):** precise Reg-24 sub-sub-section indices beyond RCAP-verified ones; the D3/2022 covering-directive body clause references (only Annexure 1 is in the repo's extracted source); the exact large-exposures return form number + submission cadence for this bank's category. No rows added, removed, or renumbered; only the two Citation/requirement cells named above and this note touched. Authority: standing register-curator mandate (Mira under Zara) + `WS-INSTRUMENT-ANALYSES` continuous workstream; CEO decision `D-ROADMAP-WS-C-RECONCILE` (approved 2026-06-07). Brief: `brief:mira:ws-instrument-analyses-drive-markets-profile-pri:2026-06-07`. (PR by Mira; stacked on the Excon v1.38 branch.)

> v1.37 — **Fit-and-proper (beneficial owners) + fit-and-proper (directors/executives supplementary) + auditor reporting obligations + returns supplement — banks-d6-2022, banks-d7-2022, banks-d2-2024, banks-d6-2025.** Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) extracts granular obligations from four SARB Prudential Authority Directive structured-JSON source files. **Source files read:** `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d6-2022-structured.json` (D6/2022 — Matters related to fit-and-proper assessment requirements pertaining to beneficial owners, 5 obligation rows); `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d7-2022-structured.json` (D7/2022 — Matters related to fit-and-proper assessment requirements pertaining to directors and executive officers supplementary, 6 obligation rows); `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d2-2024-structured.json` (D2/2024 — Reporting requirements in terms of Regulation 46 of the Regulations relating to Banks, 3 obligation rows); `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d6-2025-structured.json` (D6/2025 — Returns to be submitted to the Prudential Authority with effect from 1 July 2025, 2 obligation rows). **ID scheme:** `ORG-GV-FNP-001..005` (D6/2022 fit-and-proper beneficial-owner CBCR obligations); `ORG-GV-FNP-S-001..006` (D7/2022 supplementary fit-and-proper CBCR obligations for directors and executive officers); `ORG-PR-REP43-001..003` (D2/2024 auditor reporting requirements under Reg 46); `ORG-PR-RETURNS2-001..002` (D6/2025 supplementary returns submission obligations). **Bind triggers:** D6/2022 fit-and-proper (beneficial owners) — `LICENCE-BIND` (fit-and-proper assessments for beneficial owners are a licensing prerequisite; ongoing screening obligations bind at licence-day); D7/2022 fit-and-proper (directors/executives) — `LICENCE-BIND` (fit-and-proper CBCRs for directors and executive officers are required at bank-establishment application stage and bind at licence-day); D2/2024 auditor reporting under Reg 46 — `COMMENCEMENT-BIND` (auditor reports on BA returns arise once trading has commenced and financial-year-end reporting obligations are triggered); D6/2025 returns supplement — `COMMENCEMENT-BIND` (returns to the PA in terms of the D6/2025 supplement are triggered by commencement of trading from 1 July 2025). **Relationship to existing rows:** `ORG-PR-42` (v1.16 high-level D7/2022 CBC directors-and-executives obligation) remains; the new `ORG-GV-FNP-S-001..006` rows are granular supplements extracted from D7/2022 §2.1.1–§2.1.13 verbatim text. `ORG-PR-29` (v1.14 high-level D2/2024 Reg 46 reporting placeholder) remains; the new `ORG-PR-REP43-001..003` rows are granular supplements from D2/2024 verbatim §1–§2 text. All new rows carry `review-status: new-from-llm-extraction`. Authority: standing register-curator mandate (Mira under Zara) + `WS-INSTRUMENT-ANALYSES` continuous workstream. Brief: dispatched via Scrooge 2026-05-28. (PR by Mira; rebased on main.)

`Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d1-2022-structured.json` (D1/2022 — Liquidity Coverage Ratio: Scope of Application and Matters Related to Calculation and Disclosure, 10 obligation rows); `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d1-2023-structured.json` (D1/2023 — Matters Related to the Net Stable Funding Ratio, 12 obligation rows). **ID scheme:** `ORG-PR-P3-001..003` (D1/2024 Pillar 3 IRRBB disclosure obligations); `ORG-PR-LCR-001..010` (D1/2022 LCR scope of application and calculation obligations); `ORG-PR-NSFR-001..012` (D1/2023 NSFR calibration and implementation obligations). **Bind triggers:** D1/2024 Pillar 3 IRRBB — `COMMENCEMENT-BIND` (disclosure obligations arise when the bank has IRRBB exposures at commencement of trading; > v1.30 — **Activity scope column corrections — full-register sweep.** Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) executes a targeted correction pass on the `Activity scope` column (col 12) across all domains. No rows added or removed; no other columns touched. **Corrections made:** (1) **ORG-PR-06, ORG-PR-07, ORG-PR-08** — add `ACT-BANK-DEPOSIT` to LCR, NSFR, and intraday-liquidity rows; deposit-funding obligation rationale: these liquidity ratios exist because the bank takes deposits under Banks Act s.11. (2) **ORG-CY-01, ORG-CY-03** — add `ACT-TECH-KEY-MGMT` to cybersecurity framework and threat-modelling rows; JS 2/2024 includes cryptographic key management controls and key management is a control category in any threat-modelling / controls-catalogue exercise. (3) **ORG-CY-06** — reclassify from `ACT-TECH-CYBER, ACT-TECH-IT` → `ACT-RISK-OPERATIONAL, ACT-TECH-IT`; D3/2018 is a cloud/outsourcing governance obligation (operational risk), not a cybersecurity technical obligation; aligns with sibling ORG-CY-07. (4) **ORG-PR-61** — add `ACT-REPORT-PRUDENTIAL` to restructured-credit-exposures row; the PA directive requires not only credit classification but periodic PA reporting. (5) **ORG-PR-62** — change `ACT-GOVERN-COMPLIANCE` → `ACT-REPORT-PRUDENTIAL`; D8/2016 is a notification/reporting obligation to the PA (material outsourced service providers), not a general compliance management obligation. (6) **ORG-PR-63** — remove invalid code `ACT-TRADE-REPO` (not in the 32-code activity taxonomy) and retain `ACT-RISK-CAPITAL, ACT-REPORT-PRUDENTIAL`; the encumbered-assets obligation is capital/leverage and prudential reporting, not trade execution. (7) **ORG-PR-66** — change `ACT-REPORT-PRUDENTIAL` → `ACT-RISK-CREDIT`; D7/2020 is the SA-CCR methodology for derivative exposure in the leverage ratio — a credit/counterparty risk measurement obligation, not primarily a reporting obligation. (8) **ORG-PR-34** — add `ACT-REPORT-PRUDENTIAL`; PC 15/2024 CSRBB field-testing requires submission of field-testing returns to the PA (§4.2–§4.3). (9) **ORG-PR-40** — add `ACT-REPORT-PRUDENTIAL`; D3/2022 LEX compliance requires monthly LEX reporting to the PA (exposure caps measured daily/monthly and reported). **Domain sweep findings — no change required:** Domain B (ORG-FC-07/08/09 — `ACT-CLIENT-ONBOARD, ACT-GOVERN-COMPLIANCE` correct; reporting to FIC counts as governance-compliance, not prudential reporting); Domain C (all EXCON/FinSurv rows carry `ACT-REPORT-FINSURV` correctly); Domain F (all governance board-level rows carry `ACT-GOVERN-BOARD`); Domain J (JSE IRC rows carry `ACT-TRADE-OTC-IRD`; OTC IRD rows confirmed); Domain M (ODP rows carry `ACT-REPORT-CONDUCT` / `ACT-REPORT-TRADE` where appropriate); ORG-PR-38 (externally-facilitated liquidity stress — participation obligation, no separate PA submission required per §2.1–§2.9 text). Authority: standing register-curator mandate (Mira under Zara). Source brief: `Owner Inbox/2026-05-17_mira_obligations-register-v1.30-activity-scope-review.md`. (PR by Mira.)

> v1.23 — **Requirement column full-register cleanup — pure regulatory mandate only.** Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara CCO; obligations-register curator) executes a full-pass cleanup of column 4 (Requirement) across all domains. Three violation categories removed from every Requirement cell: (a) **entity names** — "Hoz Bank Limited", "Hoz Securities Limited", "Hoz Group Limited" replaced with generic terms ("the bank", "the controlling company", "the consolidation perimeter", "the licensed financial institution"); (b) **agent/persona names** — "Saskia", "Marc", "Atlas", "Owen", "Bea", "Zara", "Niko", "Imani", "Devon", "Iris", "Helena", and all other agent references removed; (c) **implementation detail** — "Reads alongside ORG-*" cross-references, "Discharge:" clauses, "Note:" blocks, "event-store", "substrate", "prototype/", "PR #", "D-*" decision references, multi-sentence HOW explanations, corrective-umbrella meta-commentary. **Rule enforced:** Requirement = one sentence stating WHAT the regulation mandates the bank to do — action verb + object + regulatory threshold/standard where the regulation specifies one. No actor names, no entity names, no internal system names, no cross-references, no implementation detail. **Rows cleaned (approximately 59 cells across all domains):** A (ORG-PR-33), B (ORG-FC-23), C (ORG-EXCON-*), E (ORG-CY-17), F (ORG-GV-21, ORG-GV-22), J (ORG-MK-09..16), FX (ORG-FX-01..04), M (ORG-FAIS-KI), O (ORG-GV-DIRECTORS-MINIMUM, ORG-GV-AC-MINIMUM, ORG-GV-CRO-INDEPENDENCE, ORG-GV-CFO-INDEPENDENCE, ORG-FC-11-GLOSS-CEO-MLRO-BAR, ORG-FC-MLRO-ALTERNATE, ORG-PR(IV)-13-GLOSS-DEPUTY-IO, ORG-FC-SANCTIONS-SCREENING, ORG-CY-02-RECON-CRO-INDEPENDENCE), P (ORG-FAIS-RK-ADVICE, ORG-FAIS-RK-SUITABILITY, ORG-FAIS-RK-FEE-DISCLOSURE, ORG-FAIS-RK-COMPLAINT-HANDLING, ORG-FAIS-RK-GENERAL-CODE), Q (ORG-BNK-CGPS-CONS, ORG-BNK-ICAAP-CONS, ORG-BNK-ILAAP-CONS, ORG-BNK-RECOVERY-CONS, ORG-BNK-CYBER-CONS, ORG-GRP-RPT, ORG-GRP-FINREP, ORG-GRP-PA-PARENT), RM (ORG-RM-01, ORG-RM-02, ORG-RM-03, ORG-RM-04, ORG-RM-05, ORG-RM-06). **No rows added or removed; no Citation, Fulfilment policy, Owner, Status, Entity scope, Applies-at, or Risk taxonomy cells touched; Domain N left entirely untouched (exempt from 9-column schema).** Authority: standing register-curator mandate (Mira under Zara). (5) `ORG-FAIS-RK-GENERAL-CODE` — citation now full GCC section index (s.2 general duty; s.3(1) specific duties; s.3(2) record-keeping; s.5 provider info; s.7 service info; s.8 suitability; s.9 advice record; > v1.15 — **JS number reconciliation (cybersecurity Joint Standard).** Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara CCO; obligations-register curator) closes workstream `WS-JS-NUMBER-RECONCILIATION` surfaced in v1.14 by the corrective umbrella row `ORG-CY-17` (per `Owner Inbox/2026-05-10_mira-imani_pa-communications-research.md` §6 #1). The cybersecurity-and-cyber-resilience Joint Standard is **Joint Standard 2 of 2024** (PA + FSCA, published 17 May 2024, commenced 1 June 2025), NOT Joint Standard 1 of 2024 (which is *Outsourcing by Insurers* — insurer-only, not banks). Pure rename across affected register surfaces — no rows added, no rows removed, no row-IDs changed, no schema change. **Renamed:** `ORG-CY-01..05` (Domain E — citation cells); `ORG-GV-17` (Domain F — citation cell on board approval of material policies including the cyber framework); `ORG-BNK-CYBER-CONS` (Domain Q — citation cell + body prose); `ORG-GV-CRO-INDEPENDENCE` + `ORG-CY-02-RECON-CRO-INDEPENDENCE` (Domain O — Mira+Zara responsible-person + operational-independence reading); Domain O intro paragraph (Mira+Zara JS-1-of-2024 challenge prose); Entity-scope vocabulary section — `Hoz Group Limited` description, `consolidated-supervision` table cell, Authority bullet (lines 61, 73, 100). **Domain N citation-URN inventory:** symbol `JOINT-STANDARD-1-2024-CYBER` → `JOINT-STANDARD-2-2024-CYBER`; URN slug `urn:obligation:bank:m1:operational-cyber:joint-standard-1-2024-cyber:v1` → `urn:obligation:bank:m1:operational-cyber:joint-standard-2-2024-cyber:v1`. **Code-symbol rename in substrate:** `JOINT-STANDARD-1-2024` → `JOINT-STANDARD-2-2024` propagated across 8 files (`prototype/platform/agent-identity/issuer.ts`, `permission-policy.ts`, `prototype/platform/scheduler/scheduler.ts`, `prototype/platform/event-store/registry.ts`, `prototype/platform/event-trigger-bus/bus.ts`, `prototype/platform/agent-runtime/registry.ts`, `prototype/runtime/agents/rashida-cyber-resilience-snapshot.ts`, `prototype/runtime/agents/devon-operational-resilience-snapshot.ts`, `prototype/runtime/agents/senna-security-substrate-state.ts`) + Domain N symbol propagation in `prototype/runtime/agents/mira-m1-regulator-citation-urns.ts` + recon test `prototype/tests/recon-retention-citation-coverage.test.ts`. The append-only event-store (Principle 1) retains old events with the old symbol; only newly-emitted events carry the corrected symbol — old events' citation correctness is now governed by the v1.15 rename of the register's symbol-to-meaning binding. **Banner history preserved** per established convention — prior version-banners (v1.0–v1.14) are not edited; this v1.15 banner is the rename narrative. The `JOINT-STANDARD-` prefix in the recon harness `EXTERNAL_ANCHOR_PREFIXES` list (`prototype/platform/recon/retention-citation-coverage.ts` line 112) matches both old and new symbols — no recon-config change required. **Pre-Phase-1 historical Owner Inbox files** (e.g. seed file at `Owner Inbox/2026-05-05_obligations-register-seed.md`, RAS recalibration v1) carry the old symbol and are not edited (historical record per CLAUDE.md "Records substrate" pre-Phase-1 posture; Phase 4 archive moves them to `archive/`). Authority: standing register-curator mandate (Mira under Zara) + workstream `WS-JS-NUMBER-RECONCILIATION` (the rename mandate routed in v1.14). Recorded as `CeoDecision` event of record `D-WS-JS-NUMBER-RECONCILIATION` per CLAUDE.md "Operating procedures → Events-first authoring" (event is the curator-action record, not a new authorisation). > v1.13 — **Schema unification + header reconciliation.** Closes Vera-style audit findings P1 #1 (header counts out of sync) and P1 #2 (schema split between 6-cell and 7-cell rows; `entity-scope` / `applies-at` encoded inline in the Status cell rather than as columns). Three changes: (a) **header counters reconciled to actuals** — the v1.12 banner stated `~224` total tracked obligations across `16 domains` and `~64 instruments`; actuals at the time of this PR are **232 obligations across 23 distinct domain prefixes** and `~70+` instruments. The "16 domains" reading was an artefact of an earlier state where domain count and prefix count happened to coincide; subsequent additions (Domain FX, Domain N, Domain O, Domain P, Domain Q, Domain RM) and the introduction of sub-prefixes (`BNK`, `GRP`, `CS1` / `CS2` / `CS3`, `JS2` / `JN2`, `EXCON`, `EL`, `RM`, `FAIS`) have diverged the two counts. **Decision: expand to 23 prefixes; document the taxonomy** (do not rename / consolidate, because the prefixes are already cited from substrate code — `prototype/platform/event-store/registry.ts`, `prototype/platform/recon/retention-citation-coverage.ts`, the `mira:m1-regulator-citation-urns` Domain N cross-reference column — and renames would coordinate-break across the substrate). (b) **Schema unified to 9 columns** — every obligations table row now carries `ID | URN | Citation | Requirement | Fulfilment policy | Owner | Status | Entity scope | Applies-at`. Previously, 195 rows used a 6-cell schema and 37 rows used a 7-cell schema (the 6-cell schema plus a separate `URN` column). Both are absorbed into the unified 9-column schema. **Domain N** (citation-URN inventory: `Symbol | URN slug | Tranche | Instrument | Section | First consumed at | Confidence | Cross-references`) and the **Status-summary / Entity-scope / Applies-at vocabulary tables** are explicitly exempt — they are explanatory tables, not obligations rows. (c) **Entity-scope and Applies-at promoted to first-class columns** — where the existing row encoded `entity-scope: <value>` or `applies-at: <value>` inline in the Status cell, the value is extracted into the dedicated column and the Status cell is left with the bare lifecycle status (any non-scope parenthetical tail — e.g. `**IN FORCE** (annual cycle)` or the v1.x version-marker prose — is preserved). Where no value is present, the column reads `[TBD]`. The implicit-`bank` default for rows authored before v1.8 is **not** baked into the column value; it remains `[TBD]` to make the unclassified state explicit. **Out of scope (filed as follow-on workstreams in `Owner Inbox/2026-05-10_mira_obligations-register-v1.13-reconciliation.md`):** the 57 `[citation: TBC]` markers (P1 #3, `WS-INSTRUMENT-ANALYSES`); bulk classification of entity-scope `[TBD]` placeholders (`WS-ENTITY-SCOPE-CLASSIFICATION`); applies-at `[TBD]` resolution (`WS-APPLIES-AT-CLASSIFICATION`); URN `[TBD]` population (`WS-URN-COVERAGE`); URN-form vs ORG-form decision (P2 #5); precise instrument-count deduplication (`WS-INSTRUMENT-DEDUP`). No obligations added, removed, or substantively re-stated — this is structural reconciliation only. Authority: standing register-curator mandate (Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer) under Zara (Chief Compliance Officer)); no new CEO decision required. (PR by Mira; rebased on top of v1.12.)

> **Purpose.** A consolidated register of every obligation the bank carries under applicable regulation, mapped to where it is fulfilled in the bank's policy stack. The operational expression of Principle 2 (every action traces to a source). Entries are projections over the underlying instrument analyses in this library; reproducible at any as-of date.

## How to read this register

Every obligations table row carries the same seventeen columns (per v1.31 schema update adding the four `review-*` columns; v1.25 added `Product scope` and `Activity scope`; v1.18 added `Bind-trigger`):

- **ID** — a stable register identifier `ORG-<prefix>-<n>` for cross-referencing. The 23 prefixes seen in IDs are: `AC, BNK, CD, CS1, CS2, CS3, CY, EL, EXCON, FAIS, FC, FMA, FX, GRP, GV, HR, JN2, JS2, MK, PR, RM, TX, WB`. Some prefixes carry sub-domain meaning (`BNK` and `GRP` distinguish bank-entity-with-consolidated-measurement vs group-direct rows under D-REGULATORY-PERIMETER; `CS1` / `CS2` / `CS3` track FSCA Conduct Standards 1, 2, 3 of 2018 as separate instruments; `JS2` and `JN2` track Joint Standard 2 of 2020 and Joint Notice 2 of 2024; `EXCON` carries the Excon-ODP non-resident derivative reporting row; `FAIS` and `RM` map to Domain P and Domain RM respectively).
- **URN** — machine-readable URN slug (`urn:obligation:bank:*:v1`). For rows that do not yet carry an explicit URN, this reads `[TBD]` — populating URNs across the register is a follow-on workstream `WS-URN-COVERAGE` per `Owner Inbox/2026-05-10_mira_obligations-register-v1.13-reconciliation.md`.
- **Citation** — instrument and section / clause.
- **Requirement** — plain-English statement of what the regulator requires.
- **Fulfilment policy** — where in the bank's policy library this obligation is met (cross-reference to `Owner Inbox/2026-05-06_policy-register.md` and the bundle files).
- **Owner** — accountable seat (typically a governance seat, with the engineering seat in parentheses).
- **Status** — closed 7-value enum (v1.18 rationalization): `IN_FORCE` (obligation binding and substrate satisfying it) · `DRAFTING` (active authoring / build in progress) · `PLANNED` (scheduled, not yet started) · `PARTIAL` (partially met; gap documented) · `NOT_APPLICABLE` (binding trigger not yet reached — see `Bind-trigger` column) · `DEFERRED` (intentionally deferred to a later phase) · `SUPERSEDED` (instrument superseded; row retained for audit history). Per v1.13 the Status cell no longer carries `entity-scope:` / `applies-at:` annotations. Per v1.18 the Status cell no longer carries binding-trigger annotations — those are now in the `Bind-trigger` column.
- **Bind-trigger** — one of `corporate` (binds at corporate formation), `licence-day` (binds at SARB licence grant), `commencement` (binds at commencement of trading), `conditional` (binds on a named conditional trigger — see Requirement cell), `n/a` (no deferred trigger; obligation is already live). Added v1.18.
- **Entity scope** — one of `group`, `bank`, `securities`, `multi-entity`, `consolidated-supervision`, or `[TBD]` (per the v1.8 vocabulary in the "Entity scope" section below). Rows authored before v1.8 inherited an implicit-`bank` default; v1.13 makes the unclassified state explicit by reading `[TBD]` rather than baking the implicit default into the column. Resolution sprint is workstream `WS-ENTITY-SCOPE-CLASSIFICATION`.
- **Applies-at** — one of `entity-only`, `consolidated`, `look-through`, or `[TBD]` (per the v1.9 vocabulary in the "`applies-at`" section below). Resolution sprint is workstream `WS-APPLIES-AT-CLASSIFICATION`.
- **Product scope** — pipe-separated product family codes from the canonical product taxonomy (`listed-equity`, `listed-bond`, `repo`, `otc-ird`, `fx`) or `universal` where the obligation applies across all products (e.g. governance, AML, capital adequacy, operational risk, HR, IT, corporate obligations that are not product-specific). Added v1.25.
- **Activity scope** — pipe-separated ACT-* activity codes from the locked activity vocabulary (e.g. `ACT-RISK-CAPITAL | ACT-REPORT-PRUDENTIAL`) or `universal` where the obligation applies regardless of which specific activity the bank is undertaking. Added v1.25.
- **Risk taxonomy** — primary risk-taxonomy code(s) from `Regulations/_risk-taxonomy.md` (e.g. `riskTaxonomy: RT-CR.CC`).
- **review-status** — one of the closed enum values defined in the `Review workflow` section below: `legacy-unreviewed` (default for all rows present at v1.31), `reviewed-confirmed`, `reviewed-modified`, `reviewed-retired`, `new-from-llm-extraction`. Added v1.31.
- **review-author** — name + position of the agent or seat that performed the review (e.g. `Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance)`). Empty until a review attestation is emitted. Added v1.31.
- **review-date** — ISO-8601 date the review attestation was issued. Empty until then. Added v1.31.
- **review-event-id** — UUID of the `ObligationReviewed` event that captured the attestation (see Atlas's `WS-OBLIGATION-REVIEW-SUBSTRATE` event-type set). Empty until then. Added v1.31.

**Exempt from the 17-column schema:** Domain N (the M1 markets-foundation citation-URN inventory — `Symbol | URN slug | Tranche | Instrument | Section | First consumed at | Confidence | Cross-references`) is structurally distinct from the obligations rows; it provides machine-readable handles that obligations rows cite. The Status-summary, Entity-scope-vocabulary, and Applies-at-vocabulary tables are also exempt — they are explanatory tables, not obligations rows.

Where multiple regulators converge on the same obligation, it is listed once with all relevant citations.

---

## Review workflow

> *Added 2026-05-22 (v1.31) under CEO decision `D-URN-CANONICAL-VOCABULARY` (event `3a3deae9-4274-46b0-9d0b-5208fbdb84a1`). Owen (Company Secretary, governance) curates the schema; Atlas (Core banking platform architect, engineering) lands the companion event types in `WS-OBLIGATION-REVIEW-SUBSTRATE`.*

The register is a long-lived corpus authored progressively by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) under standing curator mandate. As the bank approaches commencement-of-trading, every row needs to be reviewed by the seat with natural authority for its domain — confirming the wording, the citation, the owner, the applies-at posture, and the operational substrate that satisfies the obligation. Until a row has been reviewed it carries `review-status: legacy-unreviewed`, which is informational, not pejorative: it means the row was authored by Mira and not yet ratified by the governance seat.

### Status vocabulary

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `legacy-unreviewed` | Default state on all rows present at v1.31. Row stands as authored by Mira; no governance-seat attestation has been issued. |
| `reviewed-confirmed` | A governance seat has attested the row is correct as stated and nothing requires modification. The `review-author`, `review-date`, and `review-event-id` columns are populated. |
| `reviewed-modified` | A governance seat reviewed the row and proposed modifications; the modifications were applied (the row content reflects the post-review wording). The `review-event-id` points to the `ObligationReviewed` event that captured the proposed changes. |
| `reviewed-retired` | A governance seat reviewed the row and concluded the obligation does not bind the bank (e.g. instrument superseded, scope misclassified). The row is retained with `Status: SUPERSEDED` or `NOT_APPLICABLE`; the `review-event-id` captures the retirement reasoning. |
| `new-from-llm-extraction` | The row was added by Mira via the LLM-assisted extraction pipeline (post-v1.31) and is awaiting first review. Distinguishes "born-after-the-substrate-was-live" rows from `legacy-unreviewed` rows so the review backlog can be triaged. |

### Routing by domain — who reviews what

The routing table follows `CLAUDE.md "Decision authority routing"`: every domain maps to the seat that holds natural regulatory accountability for it. The seat may delegate the mechanical review to an engineer (e.g. Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance) delegating capital-adequacy row review to Bea (Accounting & financial reporting engineer, engineering)) but the attestation is recorded against the seat.

| Domain (`ORG-<prefix>`) | Substantive area | Reviewing seat (CEO escalation per CLAUDE.md when triggers met) |
|---|---|---|
| `PR` | Prudential — capital, liquidity, large exposures, leverage | Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance) for capital / accounting-policy rows; Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) for risk-appetite / RAS rows; Eitan (Treasurer, governance) for liquidity rows |
| `FC` | Financial crime, AML / CFT, sanctions | Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance) — CCO authority per CLAUDE.md routing table |
| `FAIS` · `CD` | Conduct, FAIS, TCF | Zara (CCO) on conduct programme; Saskia (Head of Global Markets, governance) as FAIS Key Individual |
| (privacy) | Privacy and data protection | Owen (Company Secretary, governance) as Information Officer interim; Senna (Security engineer) on technical controls |
| `CY` | Cyber and operational resilience | Devon (Chief Operating Officer, governance) on operational resilience; CISO seat (when activated) on cyber controls; CLAUDE.md CISO routing |
| `GV` · `AC` · `GRP` · `BNK` | Governance, board, corporate, consolidated supervision | Owen (Company Secretary, governance) on procedure / register / authority items; CEO on board-level items |
| (accounting) | Accounting and financial reporting | Camille (CFO) — CFO authority per CLAUDE.md routing table |
| `TX` | Tax | Camille (CFO) with Yael (Tax engineer); per CLAUDE.md CFO routing |
| `HR` | Human resources | (Build-phase: paused per `CLAUDE.md "Operating model"`; reviewed at licence-day under future CHRO seat) |
| `EXCON` · `FX` | Exchange control, FinSurv | Eitan (Treasurer, governance) with Mira (RegTech engineer) on Excon reporting |
| `MK` · `CS1` · `CS2` · `CS3` · `JN2` · `JS2` | Markets, exchange rules, conduct standards | Saskia (Head of Global Markets, governance) on market-conduct items; Zara (CCO) on FSCA conduct standards |
| `RM` | Records management (internal policy) | Owen (Company Secretary, governance) under RMS authority |
| `EL` | Legal-entity-tree items | Owen (Company Secretary, governance) with Imani (Legal-as-code engineer) |
| `WB` | Whistleblowing | Owen (Company Secretary, governance) with CCO |
| `FMA` | Financial markets infrastructure | Saskia (Head of Global Markets, governance) under indirect-participant posture |

Domain routing is the **default**; nothing prevents a row from being co-reviewed by two seats where the obligation straddles disciplines (e.g. an FX-conduct row reviewed by Saskia and Zara jointly). Co-review is captured as two `ObligationReviewed` events on the same `ObligationId`.

### Prioritisation formula

The bank cannot review 283 obligations in a single sprint. The prioritisation formula determines which `legacy-unreviewed` rows surface first in each governance seat's review queue:

> **priority = applicabilityScore × relevancyScore**

- **applicabilityScore** ∈ {0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0} — how strongly the obligation binds on the bank's current build phase. Already-live rows (`Bind-trigger: n/a`) score 1.0; `corporate`-bind rows score 1.0 (binding now); `licence-day` rows score 0.5; `commencement` rows score 0.25; `conditional` rows score 0.0 unless the conditional trigger is anticipated within the planning horizon.
- **relevancyScore** ∈ {0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0} — how directly the obligation touches the bank's chosen product / activity perimeter (FX-spot internal pre-licence test; institutional wholesale bonds, equities, OTC IRD). Rows whose `Product scope` intersects the active product perimeter score 1.0; rows on `universal` scope (governance, operational risk, etc.) score 1.0; rows on out-of-perimeter products score progressively lower; rows whose product is explicitly out-of-strategy (e.g. retail mortgages) score 0.0.

Both scores are governance-seat judgement calls captured in the `ObligationReviewProposed` event (per Atlas's `WS-OBLIGATION-REVIEW-SUBSTRATE` event type set). The product of the two surfaces high-bind, in-perimeter obligations first; deprioritises far-future or out-of-scope obligations without retiring them.

## Entity scope

> *Added 2026-05-09 (v1.8) under CEO decision `D-LEGAL-ENTITY-TREE-V0` (approved 2026-05-09; record `Owner Inbox/2026-05-09_scrooge_ceo-decision-record_d-legal-entity-tree-v0.md` / PR #82). Source: `Owner Inbox/2026-05-09_imani-owen_legal-entity-tree-v0.md` (PR #80; the entity-tree v0).*

The bank is structured as a three-entity group:

- **`Hoz Group Limited`** — non-operating parent / holding company. Subject to consolidated supervision under **Banks Act 94 of 1990 § 60+** (parent-of-bank designation, controlling-company / holding-company supervision, group capital adequacy) `§ 36 (registration as controlling company); § 70A (minimum capital and reserve funds in respect of a banking group); § 60 (directors of bank or controlling company)`, **Joint Standard 2 of 2024** (group-level cyber-resilience programme) `§ 3.2 (Banking groups — bank or controlling company must extend cyber risk management to all juristic persons structured under it)`, **BCBS Corporate Governance Principles for Banks** (group governance) `[citation: TBC — Imani/external-counsel at licence gate; Principles 1–13, several covering group/subsidiary governance]`, **IFRS 10 / IFRS 12** (consolidated financial statements + disclosure of interests in other entities), **IAS 24** (related-party disclosures at group level), **Companies Act 71 of 2008** (group-related-party regime).
- **`Hoz Bank Limited`** — regulated banking entity. Subject to the Banks Act, Regulations Relating to Banks, FIC Act 38 of 2001 (as Section 43A accountable institution), POPIA, FSR Act, BCBS Basel III/IV directly.
- **`Hoz Securities Limited`** — FSP / OTC Derivative Provider entity. Subject to FAIS Act 37 of 2002 (FSP licence + Key Individual under Determination of Fit and Proper Requirements 2017), FMA 19 of 2012 + FSCA Conduct Standards 1–3 of 2018 + Joint Standard 2 of 2020 (ODP regime), and to FIC Act as a separately-licensed Section 43A accountable institution.

Every obligation row carries an `entity-scope` field with one of these values:

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `group` | Binds **only** at the group / `Hoz Group Limited` level (e.g. consolidated capital adequacy under Banks Act § 60+; group recovery plan; consolidated financial statements under IFRS 10). |
| `bank` | Binds **only** at `Hoz Bank Limited` (e.g. SARB FinSurv reporting under the Currency and Exchanges Manual; Authorised Dealer obligations; BA returns to the Prudential Authority). |
| `securities` | Binds **only** at `Hoz Securities Limited` (e.g. FAIS Key Individual; FSP advice records / suitability assessments; FSCA conduct-of-business; ODP trade-reporting and margin under JS 2 of 2020). |
| `multi-entity` | Binds at **two or more** entities — the row enumerates which entities and how the obligation manifests at each (e.g. FIC Act Section 43A AML/CFT obligations apply to **both** `Hoz Bank Limited` and `Hoz Securities Limited` as separately-licensed accountable institutions; POPIA Information Officer designation under ss.55–56 owed **per-entity**; Companies Act 71 of 2008 director-duties bind every group entity). |
| `consolidated-supervision` | Binds at the **group level under Banks Act § 60+ AND simultaneously at each entity** — i.e. there is a group-level discharge that aggregates / consolidates the per-entity discharges, plus distinct entity-level discharges (e.g. cyber-resilience programme under Joint Standard 2 of 2024 — group programme + per-entity controls; consolidated ICAAP / ILAAP — group-level capital / liquidity adequacy + bank-entity ICAAP / ILAAP under the bank's standalone obligations). **Note (v1.9):** under D-REGULATORY-PERIMETER (PR #85, 2026-05-09), most rows previously framed as binding "at the group level under § 60+" are more precisely framed as binding on `Hoz Bank Limited` and *measured* at consolidated level via PA look-through — these rows now use `entity-scope: bank` with `applies-at: consolidated` (see below). The `consolidated-supervision` value is retained for rows where the group-level discharge is itself a separate obligation distinct from the per-entity discharge (e.g. `ORG-CY-02` per-entity vs the group cyber-resilience programme). |

### `applies-at` — where the obligation is measured / reported

> *Added 2026-05-09 (v1.9) under CEO decision `D-REGULATORY-PERIMETER` (approved 2026-05-09; record `Owner Inbox/2026-05-09_scrooge_ceo-decision-record_d-regulatory-perimeter.md` / PR #85). Source: §"What this clarifies" item 2 + §"Follow-on routes recorded" Mira route.*

The `entity-scope` field answers **on which entity the obligation binds**. The `applies-at` field answers **at what aggregation level the obligation is measured / reported / supervised**. The two are distinct because — under the regulatory-perimeter clarification — `Hoz Group Limited` is not separately regulated by the SARB PA in its own right; the PA exercises **look-through** from `Hoz Bank Limited` to assess group-wide risk. Consolidated-CGPs / ICAAP / ILAAP / recovery / cyber-resilience are obligations that **bind on the bank entity** but are **measured on a consolidated basis** including subsidiaries within scope of consolidation.

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `entity-only` | **Default.** The obligation is measured at the entity stated in `entity-scope`. No consolidation, no look-through. (e.g. SARB FinSurv reporting under the Currency and Exchanges Manual binds on `Hoz Bank Limited` and is measured at the bank entity — not consolidated.) |
| `consolidated` | The obligation binds at the entity stated in `entity-scope`, but is **measured / reported on a consolidated basis** via PA look-through. The aggregation perimeter is the group of entities within consolidation scope under Banks Act § 60+ + IFRS 10. (e.g. consolidated ICAAP binds on `Hoz Bank Limited` under PA prudential supervision but the capital adequacy ratio is computed including `Hoz Securities Limited` and any future group entity within scope.) |
| `look-through` | The SARB PA (or other regulator) reads through the entity to assess group-wide risk. The **obligation may bind on the parent (`Hoz Group Limited`) or on the entity** depending on the regulatory anchor — the citation is decisive. (e.g. parent-of-bank PA notification binds on the group; PA look-through prudential ratios bind on the bank with consolidated measurement.) |

**Default for un-classified rows.** Existing rows authored before v1.9 default to `applies-at: entity-only` (the field is not set explicitly on rows where `entity-scope: bank` and consolidation does not apply). The default is **provisional** for rows where the obligation may carry a consolidated reading; full classification of consolidated-vs-entity-only is part of the v1 substrate task already named under v1.8.

**Authority chain.** D-REGULATORY-PERIMETER refines D-LEGAL-ENTITY-TREE-V0 (PR #82) at the regulatory-perimeter layer; the `applies-at` vocabulary is the operational expression of "the bank is subject to PA prudential regulation; the group is not separately regulated; PA look-through assesses group-wide risk via the bank entity" (CEO posture statement, 2026-05-09).

---

**Default for un-classified rows.** Existing rows authored before v1.8 default to `entity-scope: bank` (the bank-entity has historically been the register's implicit subject). The default is **provisional**: full classification of the remaining ~190 rows is the v1 substrate task surfaced in `Owner Inbox/2026-05-09_mira_per-entity-register-scoping-v0.md`. The 9 sample rows in v1.8 carry an explicit `entity-scope` field; the rest remain implicit-`bank` until classified.

**Authority.** This vocabulary is the operational expression of:

- CLAUDE.md **Principle 5 — multi-entity from day one** (every event, account, position, and contract belongs to a specific legal entity in a versioned legal-entity tree).
- CLAUDE.md **Principle 2 — every action traces to a source** (each entity-scope assertion cites the regulatory anchor that justifies it).
- **Banks Act 94 of 1990 § 60+** (controlling-company / parent-of-bank consolidated-supervision regime) `§ 36 (registration as controlling company); § 70A (minimum capital and reserve funds in respect of a banking group); see also § 60 (directors of bank or controlling company) and § 73 (large exposures — group reading); precise sub-regulation indices for PA look-through on large exposures and consolidated supervision: Imani (Legal-as-code engineer) + external counsel ratify at the licence-application gate`.
- **Joint Standard 2 of 2024** on Cybersecurity and Cyber Resilience (PA / FSCA) — group-level vs entity-level discharge `§ 3.2 (Banking groups — bank or controlling company must ensure cyber risks from all juristic persons structured under it are included in the group programme; commenced 1 June 2025)`.
- **BCBS Corporate Governance Principles for Banks** (revised 2015 + subsequent amendments) — group-governance vs entity-governance `[citation: TBC — Imani/external-counsel at licence gate; the 2015 revision contains 13 principles, several of which address group and subsidiary governance explicitly (Principles 1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 9); precise group-applicability paragraph indices require text-level review of the BCBS document]`.
- **IFRS 10** (Consolidated Financial Statements), **IFRS 12** (Disclosure of Interests in Other Entities), **IAS 24** (Related Party Disclosures) — financial-reporting consolidation.
- **Companies Act 71 of 2008** — group-of-companies regime, related-party transactions, holding-company / subsidiary definitions `§ 2 (related and inter-related persons — definition); § 3 (subsidiary relationships); § 75 (related-party regime — directors with personal financial interest); precise sub-section indices (e.g. § 2(1)(a)–(c), § 3(1)(a)–(c)) to be confirmed by Imani (Legal-as-code engineer) + external counsel at the licence-application gate`.

**Operational note.** The entity-scope field is parsed by Vera (Internal audit / continuous-assurance engineer) as part of register-completeness recon: rows without an explicit entity-scope are reportable findings once the v1 classification task completes; until then, the implicit-`bank` default carries.

## Domain J — Markets, trading, market conduct

> *v1.11 — extended with the URN-slug-bearing rows the M1 completion brief promised but never landed (`ORG-MK-09..13`) plus `ORG-MK-14` covering FSCA Conduct Standard 3/2018 §§3–9 dimensional coverage (per D-NEW-PRODUCT-APPROVAL-POLICY follow-on Mira route — the §§3–9 dimensions are the FSCA-side anchor that the NPA policy's §6 14-dimension gates cite into for OTC-derivatives counterparty conduct). Existing rows `ORG-MK-01..08` retain their citation-prose form; their URN slugs are added in the new sub-table "Domain J URN slugs" below the legacy table. Authority: CEO decisions `D-PRODUCT-CONSTRUCTION-SUBSTRATE` + `D-NEW-PRODUCT-APPROVAL-POLICY` (both approved 2026-05-10). Source authorities for each new row are named inline in the row's `Citation` cell.*
>
> *URN slug pattern: `urn:obligation:bank:mk:<instrument-slug>:v1`. The `mk` infix matches the existing markets-cluster URN convention used by Domain FX FinSurv rows (`urn:obligation:bank:fx:finsurv:*:v1`). The `mk` infix in Domain J URNs distinguishes markets-trading binding (Domain J) from markets-foundation citation (Domain N's `m1` infix) — two complementary parts of the markets citation surface.*

## Domain J-IRC — Exchange Rules (JSE IRC market)

*These obligations bind at commencement of trading on the JSE IRC market. Instruments: JSE Interest Rate and Currency Rules; JSE Debt Listings Requirements; JSE Clear CCP Rules. Entity scope: bank. Applies-at: COMMENCEMENT-BIND. The bank is an indirect participant (non-clearing member per CEO decision `D-LICENCE-TYPE`) accessing the JSE IRC market via sponsor / correspondent banks where required.*

## Domain FX — FinSurv reporting (cross-border flows, Authorised Dealer)

> *Added 2026-05-09 by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer) under Zara (Chief Compliance Officer). Authority: CEO decision `D-M4-FX-SUB-DECISIONS` Sub-2 (approved 2026-05-09; record `Owner Inbox/2026-05-09_scrooge_ceo-decision-record_d-m4-fx-sub-decisions.md` / PR #54). Source proposal: `Owner Inbox/2026-05-07_saskia-kai_fx-product-family.md` §5–6. The FX product family makes the bank — as Authorised Dealer — responsible for verifying the legitimacy of every cross-border ZAR-vs-foreign-currency flow it intermediates and for reporting it to SARB FinSurv with the correct category code. This is the largest single citation extension at M4.*
>
> *Wave structure (per the approved CEO decision): wave-1 covers the high-volume current-account and capital-account categories sufficient to scope the procedure substrate at M4 build. Wave-2 (long-tail categories) carries `[citation: TBC]` placeholders and triggers at commencement-of-trading, when external-counsel / Imani verification populates the precise FinSurv category codes and Excon Manual sub-section references. Each entry below is multi-currency by definition (Principle 5): every flow type is parameterised on currency-pair, not single-currency.*
>
> *Cross-references: extends `ORG-MK-08` (Excon Manual generic) and `ORG-EXCON-ODP-001` (Excon-ODP non-resident counterparty derivative reporting) with the per-category granularity FinSurv requires. The FinSurv URN namespace is `urn:obligation:bank:fx:finsurv:<category-slug>:v1` per the canonical pattern; the `mk` infix is consistent with markets-cluster URNs (ORG-MK-* in Domain J).*

### Substrate notes — Domain FX

- **Citation-TBC items routed to Imani (Legal-as-code engineer) and external counsel.** Every wave-1 entry carries `[citation: TBC sub-section]` against the Excon Manual section and `[citation: TBC precise codes]` against the FinSurv Reporting System Manual BoP category clusters. Imani's clause-library work and the external-counsel engagement at licence-application moment will populate exact section / sub-section / category-code references. Do NOT invent codes (Principle 2).
- **Procedure substrate.** All 14 entries map to `Procedures/by-policy/finsurv-reporting.md` (planned per `2026-05-07_saskia-kai_fx-product-family.md` §6) and the FinSurv reporting system capability under `prototype/` (TBD — owned by Mira engineering, secondary to Zara CCO).
- **Multi-currency by construction (Principle 5).** Every flow type is parameterised on currency-pair; ZAR-vs-foreign is the dominant case but the obligation generalises to any cross-border flow the bank intermediates as Authorised Dealer.
- **Wave-2 trigger.** Commencement-of-trading. Until then, `wave-2-deferred` entries are placeholdered to preserve the URN namespace and decision-trail; precise citation population is the wave-2 work-package.

## Domain P — FAIS Posture A binding (D-FSP-LICENCE-NECESSITY)

> *Added 2026-05-09 by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer) under Zara (Chief Compliance Officer). Authority: CEO decision `D-FSP-LICENCE-NECESSITY` (resolved 2026-05-09 as `confirm-A-no-research`; record `Owner Inbox/2026-05-09_scrooge_ceo-decision-record_d-fsp-licence-necessity-confirm-a.md` / PR #62). Source proposal: `Owner Inbox/2026-05-09_zara_fsp-application-path.md` (PR #44). Posture A is the steady-state, not the interim: the bank pursues an FSP licence under FAIS Act 37 of 2002; Saskia (Head of Global Markets, governance) is the steady-state FAIS Key Individual under the Determination of Fit and Proper Requirements 2017; Marc remains FAIS KI interim until Saskia's fit-and-proper file completes (Saskia's PR #45 Gate (b)). FAIS Categories I and II likely sufficient for the institutional-only product set; counsel ratifies at the licence-application gate. The FAIS-record-keeping substrate is binding from corporate-formation: advice records, suitability assessments, fee disclosures, complaint-handling, General Code of Conduct umbrella.*
>
> *Each row carries a stable URN under `urn:obligation:bank:fais:*:v1`. Status is `corporate-bind` per `project_rules_bind_at_commencement.md` — the obligation binds at corporate formation (the bank as accountable institution / future FSP) rather than waiting for licence-day. The `[citation: TBC]` flags below are the General Code of Conduct sub-section references; Imani (Legal-as-code engineer) and external counsel ratify the precise references at the licence-application gate (per `Owner Inbox/2026-05-09_imani_external-counsel-licence-application.md`). Do NOT invent General Code section refs (Principle 2).*
>
> *Cross-references: extends Domain C parents `ORG-CD-02` (FSP licence required), `ORG-CD-03` (Key Individuals), `ORG-CD-04` (advice records suitability), `ORG-CD-06` (fee disclosure), `ORG-CD-07` (complaints handling) — Domain P resolves the FSP-conditional flags those parents carried while Posture A was unresolved. Reconciles with `ORG-FAIS-KI` (gap row from `D-THIN-HUMAN-LAYER-MINIMUM` follow-on, PR #42) — Domain P closes that row's gap-status under the confirmed Posture A reading.*

## Domain O — Thin human layer obligations (D-THIN-HUMAN-LAYER-MINIMUM)

> *Added 2026-05-09 by Mira on the back of CEO decision `D-THIN-HUMAN-LAYER-MINIMUM` (approved 2026-05-08, modified Owen+Imani's drafted composition from 5→6 separate humans + Marc + audit firm; adopts Mira+Zara's Joint Standard 2 of 2024 challenge that interim-CRO-as-CEO is a structural finding — the original Mira+Zara research cited "JS-1-of-2024" prior to the v1.15 reconciliation; the underlying instrument is and always was Joint Standard 2 of 2024 (cybersecurity-and-cyber-resilience), per `ORG-CY-17` corrective umbrella row landed in v1.14). Source: `Owner Inbox/2026-05-09_mira-zara_concentration-risk-conduct-confirmation.md` §5 (gap list of 10) + `Owner Inbox/2026-05-08_scrooge_ceo-decision-record_d-thin-human-layer-minimum.md` (follow-on route to Mira). These rows harden the licence-day human-roster minima — director count, AC composition, CRO and CFO independence, MLRO-alternate, deputy-IO, FAIS Key Individual, sanctions-screening operational accountability — and reconcile three existing rows (ORG-FC-11, ORG-PR(IV)-13, ORG-CY-02) against the JS-2-of-2024 + FIC-published-guidance reading. Each row carries a stable URN under the canonical `urn:obligation:bank:` scheme defined in `prototype/platform/citation/types.ts`; the URN is the machine-readable handle (event-emit, Vera-recon-input). The `ORG-*` ID is the human-readable handle (markdown register, dashboard view).*

## Domain RM — Internal-policy citation handles (records management, operational-substrate retention)

> *Added 2026-05-10 (v1.12) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer) under Zara (Chief Compliance Officer). Authority: CEO decision `D-PRODUCT-CONSTRUCTION-SUBSTRATE` (approved 2026-05-10) Slice 3 follow-on Mira route. **Domain-RM is structurally distinct from the regulator-instrument domains (A–Q)** — it carries internal-policy citation handles for the bank's own policies-of-record, primarily the Records Management Policy. The pattern: when an obligation register entry needs to cite an *internal* policy rather than a regulator instrument, it does so via a Domain-RM URN that resolves to the policy register (`Owner Inbox/2026-05-06_policy-register.md`) rather than to a regulation library entry. This preserves the Principle 2 single-graph discipline: the substrate code can cite a stable URN handle even while the underlying policy is `PLANNED` — the URN is the canonical handle; the policy's content fills in once authored.*
>
> *Per **Principle 6 — autonomous by default**: the Records Management Policy is presently `PLANNED` in the policy register (Owen's planned Board-approval cadence); Domain RM is the citation surface that bridges the substrate's retention-class declarations to the policy's eventual authoring. **Substrate gap surfaced here:** Records Management Policy is `PLANNED` and currently has no authored content — the URN handle below is a citation seat, not yet a citation against authored policy text. Owen is the named policy owner; the next-run authoring cycle is on Owen's standing-agent cadence per `Team/Owen.md` §6 (Cadence) + §11 (Outputs).*

## Domain Q — Group-entity Companies Act + PA-look-through-consolidated obligations (under D-LEGAL-ENTITY-TREE-V0 / D-REGULATORY-PERIMETER)

> *Added 2026-05-09 (v1.8) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer) under Zara (Chief Compliance Officer). **Reclassified 2026-05-09 (v1.9) under CEO decision `D-REGULATORY-PERIMETER` (approved 2026-05-09; record `Owner Inbox/2026-05-09_scrooge_ceo-decision-record_d-regulatory-perimeter.md` / PR #85)** — see header changelog. Original authority: CEO decision `D-LEGAL-ENTITY-TREE-V0` (approved 2026-05-09; record `Owner Inbox/2026-05-09_scrooge_ceo-decision-record_d-legal-entity-tree-v0.md` / PR #82). Source: entity-tree v0 deliverable `Owner Inbox/2026-05-09_imani-owen_legal-entity-tree-v0.md` (PR #80) + group-structure decision record `Owner Inbox/2026-05-09_scrooge_ceo-decision-record_d-group-structure.md` (PR #78). The bank's three-entity group structure — `Hoz Group Limited` (parent) + `Hoz Bank Limited` (regulated bank) + `Hoz Securities Limited` (FSP / OTC Derivative Provider) — triggers two distinct sets of obligations: (a) those binding on `Hoz Group Limited` as a Companies Act / IFRS / IAS entity in its own right (3 rows — `entity-scope: group`); (b) those binding on `Hoz Bank Limited` but measured at consolidated level via PA look-through (5 rows — `entity-scope: bank`, `applies-at: consolidated`). The CEO-clarified perimeter is: bank → SARB PA, securities → JSE-primary, group → not separately regulated, Companies Act + PA look-through only.*
>
> *Status `corporate-bind` per `project_rules_bind_at_commencement.md` — these obligations bind at corporate formation of the group, not at licence-day or commencement-of-trading. The substrate (consolidated capital, consolidated liquidity, consolidated cyber programme, group recovery plan, group-level financial statements) is built into the platform from the outset under Principle 5 (multi-entity from day one).*
>
> *Each row carries a stable URN under `urn:obligation:bank:group:*:v1`. `[citation: TBC]` flags mark precise sub-section references (Banks Act § 60+ exact paragraphs, Joint Standard 2 of 2024 specific clauses, BCBS Principle numbers, Companies Act 71 of 2008 exact sections); Imani (Legal-as-code engineer) + external counsel ratify at the licence-application gate. Do NOT invent precise references (Principle 2).*
>
> *Cross-references: extends Domain A (`ORG-PR-01..05` consolidated capital reading), Domain A (`ORG-PR-06..08` consolidated liquidity reading), Domain G operational-cyber (`ORG-CY-02` per-entity counterpart of consolidated cyber-resilience), Domain H accounting (consolidated financial reporting), Domain B financial crime (group-level AML governance under Banks Act § 60+).*

Approximate per-status counts above (~158 IN FORCE, ~19 PARTIAL, etc.) are unchanged by v1.13.

## Maintenance

Mira (compliance / RegTech engineer) curates this register under Zara (CCO).

- Each instrument analysis file in this library produces a slice of register entries.
- New entries are events: `ObligationRegistered { id, citation, requirement, fulfilment, owner, status, version }`.
- Status changes are events; `ObligationStatusChanged { id, from, to, reason, citation }`.
- Vera (audit) consumes the register's status events as continuous-controls evidence.
- Quarterly review by the Interim Risk Forum; annual review by the Audit Committee (Interim Audit Forum) once a Board exists, the BRC and AC.
- Regulatory-change management feeds amendments — new obligations are added; superseded obligations are retired with date and reason.

## Co-dependencies

- `Owner Inbox/2026-05-06_policy-register.md` — the policy library this register fulfils.
- `Owner Inbox/2026-05-06_governance-framework.md` — the constitutional document under which this register operates.
- `Owner Inbox/2026-05-06_core-policies-*.md` — the core policy bundles whose contents discharge each obligation.
- `Regulations/_index.md` — the instrument inventory; per-instrument analyses extend the register.

## Domain M-ODP-AUTH — ODP Authorisation Criteria (CS 1/2018)

> *Added 2026-05-28 (v1.33) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) via structured-JSON extraction of `Regulations/ODP/source-docs/cs-1-2018-structured.json`. CS 1/2018 is issued under FMA s.6(8)(a); its criteria bind at the point of ODP authorisation application and continue as ongoing obligations. All rows: `Bind-trigger: licence-day`, `Entity scope: securities` (Hoz Securities Limited as the ODP entity), `review-status: new-from-llm-extraction`.*

| ID | URN | Citation | Requirement | Fulfilment policy | Owner | Status | Bind-trigger | Entity scope | Applies-at | Product scope | Activity scope | Risk taxonomy | review-status | review-author | review-date | review-event-id |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ORG-ODP-AUTH-001 | [TBD] | CS 1/2018 §4.1(1)(a)–(e) + §4.1(2)(a) | Maintain capital, retained earnings and reserves proportional to ODP business risks; hold sufficient equity-funded liquid net assets to cover at least six months of operating expenses and to ensure an orderly wind-up of at least six months under stress scenarios; safeguard assets at supervised regulated entities; maintain a controlling-body-approved plan for raising additional equity if capital falls below the required level; and furnish the FSCA with a quarterly report detailing the liquid-net-asset calculation. | Capital Management Policy; ICAAP; ODP Authorisation Policy | Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance); Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance) secondary | NOT_APPLICABLE | licence-day | securities | entity-only | interest-rate-derivatives, fx-instruments | ACT-RISK-CAPITAL, ACT-REPORT-CONDUCT | riskTaxonomy: RT-CR, RT-LR.RC | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-ODP-AUTH-002 | [TBD] | CS 1/2018 §4.2.1(1)–(5) | Ensure every member of the controlling body and every senior manager is honest and has integrity; disclose proactively all relevant facts to the FSCA; submit completed Annexure B and C Forms in respect of each controlling-body member and senior manager; and continuously satisfy the FSCA that none of the disqualifying factors in §4.2.1(3)(a)–(f) apply (criminal/civil findings of dishonesty, professional-body sanctions, regulatory-body sanctions, licence suspension/withdrawal, court-ordered disqualification from company management). | Fit-and-Proper Policy; Governance Framework | Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance); Owen (Company Secretary, governance) | NOT_APPLICABLE | licence-day | securities | entity-only | interest-rate-derivatives, fx-instruments | ACT-GOVERN-COMPLIANCE, ACT-CORP-EMPLOYEE | riskTaxonomy: RT-ST.GV | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-ODP-AUTH-003 | [TBD] | CS 1/2018 §4.2.2(1)–(4) | Satisfy the FSCA that controlling-body members and senior managers have the competency to undertake their duties and functions, including detailed knowledge of relevant OTC derivative structures, risks and markets; maintain an updated register of controlling-body members and senior managers; promptly notify the FSCA on termination of any appointment and the reason; submit Annexure B for each new appointment promptly; and annually submit a written statement to the FSCA confirming continued compliance with CS 1/2018 in respect of each controlling-body member and senior manager. | Fit-and-Proper Policy; Competency Framework; ODP Authorisation Policy | Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance); Owen (Company Secretary, governance) | NOT_APPLICABLE | licence-day | securities | entity-only | interest-rate-derivatives, fx-instruments | ACT-GOVERN-COMPLIANCE, ACT-CORP-EMPLOYEE | riskTaxonomy: RT-ST.GV | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-ODP-AUTH-004 | [TBD] | CS 1/2018 §4.2.3(a)–(d) | Maintain the operational ability to fulfil all FMA responsibilities, including: a fixed business address in South Africa; adequate access to communication facilities including a full-time telephone or cell-phone service and administrative and electronic facilities; adequate storage and filing systems for safe-keeping of records and correspondence; and a bank account with a registered South African bank. | ODP Authorisation Policy; Operational Resilience Policy | Devon (Chief Operating Officer, governance); Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance) | NOT_APPLICABLE | licence-day | securities | entity-only | interest-rate-derivatives, fx-instruments | ACT-RISK-OPERATIONAL, ACT-TECH-IT | riskTaxonomy: RT-OP | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-ODP-AUTH-005 | [TBD] | CS 1/2018 §4.2.4(1)–(3) + §4.2.5 | At all times: not be in liquidation, provisional liquidation, business rescue or creditor-arrangement; maintain assets (excluding goodwill and intangibles) exceeding liabilities (excluding validly subordinated loans); maintain current assets at least sufficient to meet current liabilities; not have any outstanding judgment debt; and ensure no controlling-body member or senior manager is an unrehabilitated insolvent, under debt review, or has an outstanding judgment debt. | Governance Framework; Capital Management Policy | Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance); Owen (Company Secretary, governance) | NOT_APPLICABLE | licence-day | securities | entity-only | interest-rate-derivatives, fx-instruments | ACT-RISK-CAPITAL, ACT-GOVERN-COMPLIANCE | riskTaxonomy: RT-CR, RT-ST.GV | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-ODP-AUTH-006 | [TBD] | CS 1/2018 §4.3(1)–(9) + §4.4(a)–(d) + §4.5 + §4.6(a)–(e) | Organise and control affairs responsibly and effectively with sound risk-management principles and board-approved risk policies and processes; conduct an independent review of the risk-measurement system at least annually; maintain a compliance function staffed with competent individuals sufficiently independent from monitored activities; appoint a Head of Compliance responsible for the compliance function, FSCA reporting (annual and ad hoc on non-compliance and material irregularities), and oversight of FMA compliance; notify the FSCA of the Head of Compliance appointment; maintain a business continuity plan and regularly test it; and maintain proper, complete, accurate and secure records of all OTC derivative transaction instructions for at least six months after the instruction and of all contractual arrangements for at least five years after termination. | Risk Management Framework; Compliance Policy; Business Continuity Plan; Records Management Policy | Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance); Devon (Chief Operating Officer, governance) secondary; Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) secondary | NOT_APPLICABLE | licence-day | securities | entity-only | interest-rate-derivatives, fx-instruments | ACT-RISK-OPERATIONAL, ACT-GOVERN-COMPLIANCE, ACT-TECH-DATA | riskTaxonomy: RT-OP, RT-LR.RC | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
