## Domain M-ODP-COND — ODP Conduct Obligations (CS 2/2018)

> *Added 2026-05-28 (v1.33) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) via structured-JSON extraction of `Regulations/ODP/source-docs/cs-2-2018-structured.json`. CS 2/2018 is issued under FMA s.6(8)(b), read with ss.74 and 75; it is binding on all authorised ODPs from the date of authorisation (compliance within six months). All rows: `Bind-trigger: commencement`, `Entity scope: securities`, `review-status: new-from-llm-extraction`.*

| ID | URN | Citation | Requirement | Fulfilment policy | Owner | Status | Bind-trigger | Entity scope | Applies-at | Product scope | Activity scope | Risk taxonomy | review-status | review-author | review-date | review-event-id |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ORG-ODP-COND-001 | [TBD] | CS 2/2018 §3(a)–(g) | Act at all times honestly, fairly, with due skill, care and diligence, and in the interests of clients, counterparties and market integrity; communicate information in a clear, fair and non-misleading way; manage conflicts of interest fairly; observe proper standards of market conduct; not impede the objects of the FMA; maintain knowledge of and comply with all applicable laws, rules and regulations; and engage with the FSCA in an open and cooperative way, disclosing any material non-compliance or material operational failure. | Market Conduct Policy; Conflicts of Interest Policy; OTC Trading Policy | Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance); Saskia (Head of Global Markets, governance) secondary | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | securities | entity-only | interest-rate-derivatives, fx-instruments | ACT-TRADE-OTC-IRD, ACT-TRADE-FX, ACT-GOVERN-COMPLIANCE | riskTaxonomy: RT-CD.CC, RT-CD.CI | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-ODP-COND-002 | [TBD] | CS 2/2018 §4(1)–(7) | Prior to entering into any OTC derivative transaction, categorise each person as counterparty or client; where a person meets the counterparty definition notify them in writing of their categorisation and its limitations; obtain written confirmation of awareness and agreement; assess appropriateness of counterparty categorisation where client elects up-categorisation; warn clients in writing of the implications of counterparty categorisation and clearing obligations; and re-categorise a counterparty back to client if they no longer meet counterparty requirements. | Client Categorisation Policy; Counterparty Onboarding Policy | Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance); Saskia (Head of Global Markets, governance) secondary | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | securities | entity-only | interest-rate-derivatives, fx-instruments | ACT-CLIENT-CATEGORISE, ACT-CLIENT-ONBOARD | riskTaxonomy: RT-CD.CC | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-ODP-COND-003 | [TBD] | CS 2/2018 §5(1)–(6) | Prior to executing an OTC derivative transaction with a client, request information regarding the client's financial situation, objectives, knowledge and experience in OTC derivatives to assess appropriateness for that product type; if the product is not appropriate notify the client in writing; if the client does not provide sufficient information warn the client in writing that appropriateness cannot be determined; maintain written policies and procedures for the appropriateness assessment. | Appropriateness Assessment Policy; Client Categorisation Policy | Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance); Saskia (Head of Global Markets, governance) secondary | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | securities | entity-only | interest-rate-derivatives, fx-instruments | ACT-CLIENT-CATEGORISE, ACT-CLIENT-ONBOARD, ACT-CLIENT-ADVICE | riskTaxonomy: RT-CD.CC | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-ODP-COND-004 | [TBD] | CS 2/2018 §6(1)–(4) | Ensure all representations and information provided to clients are factually correct, in plain language, adequate and appropriate for the transaction or service, provided in good time before execution, in clear and readable format where written, and with all monetary amounts stated in specific monetary terms; provide material or significant changes in information without delay; provide a general explanation of the nature, material terms, restrictions, conditions and risks of each transaction sufficient for an informed client decision. | Client Disclosure Policy; OTC Trading Policy | Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance); Saskia (Head of Global Markets, governance) secondary | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | securities | entity-only | interest-rate-derivatives, fx-instruments | ACT-CLIENT-ADVICE, ACT-TRADE-OTC-IRD, ACT-TRADE-FX | riskTaxonomy: RT-CD.CC | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-ODP-COND-005 | [TBD] | CS 2/2018 §7(1)–(5) | Enter into a written or electronic agreement with every client or counterparty covering: payment obligations, netting of payments, events of default and other termination events, calculation and netting of obligations upon termination and transfer, governing law, valuation of transactions, collateral and portfolios, and dispute resolution; the agreement must also make provision for confirmation arrangements (§8), credit-support arrangements including margin requirements and eligible collateral, collateral-safeguarding arrangements, counterparty-status attestation and notification obligations, client consent to trade-repository reporting, and the transaction-valuation process; maintain a record of all agreements and make them promptly available to the FSCA on request. | Counterparty Onboarding Policy; Imani clause library; Collateral Management Policy | Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance); Imani (Legal-as-code engineer, engineering) secondary | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | securities | entity-only | interest-rate-derivatives, fx-instruments | ACT-TRADE-OTC-IRD, ACT-TRADE-FX, ACT-CLIENT-ONBOARD, ACT-CORP-LEGAL | riskTaxonomy: RT-LR.CT | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-ODP-COND-006 | [TBD] | CS 2/2018 §8(1)–(8) | Use best efforts to confirm every OTC derivative transaction by the end of the next business day for standard products or within five business days for complex products (per the schedule in Annexure A); maintain written policies and procedures for timely confirmation; make and retain records of the date and time of each transaction-supplement transmission and each confirmation; and provide the FSCA with a monthly report within 10 business days of month-end setting out, per product type and client or counterparty, the number of transactions not confirmed within the Annexure A timelines. | Confirmation Procedure; OTC Trading Policy; Trade Reporting Policy | Saskia (Head of Global Markets, governance); Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance) secondary | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | securities | entity-only | interest-rate-derivatives, fx-instruments | ACT-TRADE-OTC-IRD, ACT-TRADE-FX, ACT-REPORT-CONDUCT | riskTaxonomy: RT-OP.PR, RT-LR.RC | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-ODP-COND-007 | [TBD] | CS 2/2018 §9(1)–(5) | Agree in writing with counterparties and clients on portfolio-reconciliation arrangements before entering into any OTC derivative contract; perform portfolio reconciliation at the frequency prescribed by portfolio size (counterparty: daily ≥500 trades, weekly 51–499, quarterly 1–50; client: quarterly ≥101, annually 1–100); maintain written policies and procedures for portfolio reconciliation; resolve material-term discrepancies within three business days and valuation discrepancies within five business days; deem unresolved discrepancies as disputes after those periods. | Portfolio Reconciliation Procedure; OTC Trading Policy | Tomas (payments/settlement engineer, engineering); Saskia (Head of Global Markets, governance) secondary | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | securities | entity-only | interest-rate-derivatives, fx-instruments | ACT-TRADE-OTC-IRD, ACT-TRADE-FX, ACT-RISK-CREDIT | riskTaxonomy: RT-OP.PR, RT-CR.CP | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

## Domain M-ODP-RPT — ODP Trade Reporting (CS 3/2018)

> *Added 2026-05-28 (v1.33) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) via structured-JSON extraction of `Regulations/ODP/source-docs/cs-3-2018-structured.json`. CS 3/2018 is issued under FMA s.58 read with FMA Regulation 3; it prescribes trade-reporting obligations for all authorised ODPs and CCPs. Strate (Pty) Ltd is the licensed trade repository (licensed December 2024; live 1 March 2027). Reporting start date is six months after the effective date of the Standard (determined by the Authority). ## Domain A-BCIII — Basel III Implementation Roadmap (D12/2025)

> *Added 2026-05-28 (v1.34) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) via structured-JSON extraction of `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d12-2025-structured.json`. D12/2025 is issued by the SARB Prudential Authority under Banks Act s.6(6)(a). It applies to all banks, branches of foreign institutions, and controlling companies registered or operating in South Africa. It reaffirms the implementation roadmap for Basel III post-crisis reforms and the directive on distressed restructured credit exposures. The Basel III capital requirements bind at commencement of trading. The acknowledgement-of-receipt obligation binds immediately on receipt of the directive. All rows: `review-status: new-from-llm-extraction`.*

| ID | URN | Citation | Requirement | Fulfilment policy | Owner | Status | Bind-trigger | Entity scope | Applies-at | Product scope | Activity scope | Risk taxonomy | review-status | review-author | review-date | review-event-id |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Directive remains effective as confirmed in Banks Act Circular 1 of the relevant year per §1.3. Enabling authority: Banks Act 94/1990 s.6(6)(a). | Ensure compliance with all requirements set out in Directive D12/2025, including the implementation of Basel III post-crisis reforms, in accordance with the roadmap timelines confirmed by the Prudential Authority; ensure the institution remains aligned with the SARB Basel III implementation schedule as updated annually. | Capital Management Policy; Basel III Implementation Programme; `Procedures/by-policy/capital-framework-basel-iii.md` (planned; relates to `ORG-PR-31` G3/2023 Basel implementation dates + `ORG-PR-33` PC18/2024 FRTB+CVA roadmap) | Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance) — primary; Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — risk-appetite dimension | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | multi-asset | ACT-RISK-CAPITAL, ACT-REPORT-PRUDENTIAL | riskTaxonomy: RT-CR, RT-LR.RC | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

## Domain A-SACCR — SA-CCR Granular Obligations (D7/2020)

> *Added 2026-05-28 (v1.34) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) via structured-JSON extraction of `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d7-2020-structured.json`. D7/2020 is issued under Banks Act s.6(6)(a). It replaces the Current Exposure Method (CEM) with the Standardised Approach for Counterparty Credit Risk (SA-CCR) for computing derivative exposures in the leverage ratio. D7/2020 §2.7 specifies that banks must comply simultaneously with the implementation of the amended Regulations incorporating the SA-CCR methodology. These rows supplement the high-level `ORG-PR-66` placeholder (v1.29) with granular obligations extracted from the verbatim directive text. All rows: `Bind-trigger: commencement`, `Entity scope: bank`, `review-status: new-from-llm-extraction`.*

| ID | URN | Citation | Requirement | Fulfilment policy | Owner | Status | Bind-trigger | Entity scope | Applies-at | Product scope | Activity scope | Risk taxonomy | review-status | review-author | review-date | review-event-id |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ORG-PR-SACCR-001 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d7-2020-saccr-core-formula:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D7/2020** §2.1 — Core SA-CCR exposure formula. `§2.1: banks are hereby directed to calculate its exposures associated with all derivative transactions, including where a bank sells protection using a credit derivative, through the application of the formula: Exposure amount = alpha × (RC + PFE), where alpha = 1.4; RC = replacement cost; PFE = potential future exposure.` Enabling authority: Banks Act 94/1990 s.6(6)(a); Regulations Relating to Banks Reg 38A (leverage ratio). | Calculate the leverage ratio derivative exposure amount for all derivative transactions using the SA-CCR formula: Exposure = 1.4 × (RC + PFE), where RC is the replacement cost and PFE is the potential future exposure, as directed by D7/2020 §2.1 simultaneously with implementation of the amended Regulations. | Capital Management Policy; Market Risk Policy; `Procedures/by-policy/leverage-ratio-sa-ccr.md` (planned) | Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance) + Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance); Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — SA-CCR computation engine | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | entity-only | otc-ird | fx | ACT-RISK-CAPITAL, ACT-RISK-CREDIT | riskTaxonomy: RT-MK, RT-CR.CP | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-SACCR-002 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d7-2020-saccr-netting-set:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D7/2020** §2.2.1 — Netting-set exposure calculation. `§2.2.1: In the case of a derivative exposure covered by an eligible bilateral netting contract or agreement that complies with the relevant requirements specified in regulation 23(18)(b) of the amended Regulations that incorporated the requirements related to the SA-CCR, the bank shall calculate its exposure in respect of the relevant set of derivative exposures covered by the said contract or agreement as the sum of the net mark-to-market replacement cost, plus the PFE as determined.` Regulations Relating to Banks Reg 23(18)(b) (bilateral netting eligibility). | Where derivative exposures are covered by an eligible bilateral netting contract complying with Reg 23(18)(b), calculate the exposure for the netting set as the sum of net mark-to-market replacement cost plus PFE, applying the SA-CCR netting methodology. | Capital Management Policy; Collateral Management Policy; Netting Legal Framework | Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance); Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — netting-set engine; Imani (Legal-as-code engineer, engineering) — netting agreement legal eligibility | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | entity-only | otc-ird | fx | ACT-RISK-CAPITAL, ACT-RISK-CREDIT, ACT-CORP-LEGAL | riskTaxonomy: RT-CR.CP | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-SACCR-003 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d7-2020-saccr-collateral-no-netting:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D7/2020** §2.3.1 — Prohibition on netting collateral received against leverage exposure. `§2.3.1: Since collateral received in respect of any derivative contract does not necessarily reduce the leverage inherent in a bank's derivatives position, the bank shall not apply any netting between the collateral received and the bank's derivative exposure, irrespective of whether or not netting may be permitted in terms of the bank's operative accounting or risk-based framework provided for in the Regulations; whenever the bank calculates its relevant exposure amount, the bank shall not reduce the exposure amount by any collateral received from the counterparty.` | When calculating the leverage ratio derivative exposure amount, not reduce the exposure amount by any collateral received from the counterparty, regardless of whether netting is permitted under the bank's accounting or risk-based framework. | Capital Management Policy; Collateral Management Policy; `Procedures/by-policy/leverage-ratio-sa-ccr.md` (planned) | Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance); Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — leverage-ratio computation | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | entity-only | otc-ird | fx | ACT-RISK-CAPITAL | riskTaxonomy: RT-CR.CP | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-SACCR-004 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d7-2020-saccr-collateral-gross-up:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D7/2020** §2.3.2 — Gross-up for collateral provided. `§2.3.2: Whenever the bank provides collateral, the bank shall gross up its relevant exposure measure by the amount of any derivatives collateral so provided when the provision of such collateral reduces the value of the bank's balance sheet assets in terms of its relevant operative accounting framework.` | When the bank provides derivatives collateral that reduces the value of balance sheet assets under its operative accounting framework, gross up the leverage ratio exposure measure by the amount of collateral provided. | Capital Management Policy; Collateral Management Policy | Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance); Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | entity-only | otc-ird | fx | ACT-RISK-CAPITAL | riskTaxonomy: RT-CR.CP | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-SACCR-005 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d7-2020-saccr-cash-variation-margin:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D7/2020** §2.4.2 — Cash variation margin treatment. `§2.4.2: below are met, the bank — (a) may regard the cash portion of any variation margin exchanged between counterparties as a form of pre-settlement payment; and (b) may reduce the replacement cost portion of the exposure measure with the cash portion of the variation margin received and the bank may deduct from the exposure measure the receivable asset(s) in respect of the cash variation margin provided: (i) in the case of cash variation margin received, the receiving bank may reduce the replacement cost, but not the PFE, of the exposure amount of the derivative asset by the amount of cash received if the positive mark-to-market value has not already been reduced by the same amount; and (ii) in the case of cash variation margin provided to a counterparty, the posting bank may deduct any resulting receivable from its relevant exposure measure.` | Apply the prescribed conditions for treating cash variation margin as a pre-settlement payment: reduce replacement cost (not PFE) by cash variation margin received; deduct the receivable for cash variation margin provided; apply the prescribed eligibility conditions for this treatment as set out in D7/2020. | Capital Management Policy; Collateral Management Policy; Margin Policy | Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance); Eitan (Treasurer, governance) — margin flows; Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — RC/PFE engine | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | entity-only | otc-ird | fx | ACT-RISK-CAPITAL, ACT-RISK-CREDIT | riskTaxonomy: RT-CR.CP | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-SACCR-006 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d7-2020-saccr-clearing-member:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D7/2020** §2.5.1 — Clearing-member exposure treatment. `§2.5.1: When the bank acts as a clearing member and offers clearing services to clients: (a) where the bank is obliged to reimburse a client for losses if CCP defaults, capture all relevant trade exposures to the CCP similarly to other derivatives, including variation margin in the exposure amount whether or not insolvency-remote; (b) where the bank has no such obligation for QCCP defaults, may exclude relevant trade exposure amounts; (c) where the bank guarantees client derivative exposures to the CCP, calculate related leverage ratio exposure as a derivative exposure as if directly entered with the client.` | Where the bank acts as a clearing member, apply the prescribed treatment for trade exposures to CCPs and client-clearing guarantees in the leverage ratio exposure measure per D7/2020 §2.5.1. | Capital Management Policy; Clearing & Settlement Policy | Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance); Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering); Tomas (payments/settlement engineer, engineering) | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | entity-only | otc-ird | ACT-RISK-CAPITAL, ACT-RISK-CREDIT | riskTaxonomy: RT-CR.CP | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-SACCR-007 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d7-2020-saccr-written-credit-derivative-notional:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D7/2020** §2.6.1 — Written credit derivative notional inclusion. `§2.6.1: Since a written credit derivative instrument creates a notional credit exposure that arises from the creditworthiness of the relevant reference entity, a bank shall, in addition to the CCR exposure arising from the fair value of the relevant contract and any related collateral, treat any written credit derivative instrument consistently with cash instruments, such as loans or bonds, for the purposes of the bank's exposure measure.` + §2.6.2: Include in the exposure measure the effective notional amount referenced by the written credit derivative instrument, reducible by negative fair-value changes reflected in Tier 1 capital. | For written credit derivative instruments, include the effective notional amount in the leverage ratio exposure measure in addition to the CCR exposure from fair value, treating written credit derivatives consistently with cash instruments such as loans or bonds. | Capital Management Policy; Market Risk Policy; Credit Risk Policy | Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance); Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — notional inclusion in leverage engine | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | entity-only | otc-ird | ACT-RISK-CAPITAL, ACT-RISK-CREDIT | riskTaxonomy: RT-CR.CP, RT-MK | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-SACCR-008 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d7-2020-saccr-written-credit-derivative-offset:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D7/2020** §2.6.3 — Purchased credit derivative offset against written credit derivative. `§2.6.3: The bank may reduce the resulting amount by the effective notional amount of a purchased credit derivative instrument on the reference name, provided: (a) same/identical reference name; (b) remaining maturity of protection purchased ≥ remaining maturity of written instrument; (c) effective notional of offsetting purchased credit protection reduced by positive fair-value change in Tier 1; (d) for single-name credit derivatives: protection purchased on pari-passu-or-junior obligation; (e) protection purchased on a pool may offset sold protection on individual names only if economically equivalent to buying separately on each name; (f) partial-pool protection — no offsetting permitted for protection sold on individual reference names.` | Apply the permitted offsets for purchased credit protection against written credit derivative exposure in the leverage ratio, subject to the reference-entity identity, maturity, seniority, and economic-equivalence conditions set out in D7/2020 §2.6.3. | Capital Management Policy; Credit Risk Policy; Hedging Policy | Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance); Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — offset eligibility engine | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | entity-only | otc-ird | ACT-RISK-CAPITAL, ACT-RISK-CREDIT | riskTaxonomy: RT-CR.CP | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-SACCR-009 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d7-2020-saccr-trs-no-offset:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D7/2020** §2.6.4 — Total return swap credit protection not recognised. `§2.6.4: When the bank buys credit protection through a total return swap (TRS) and records the net payments received as net income, but does not record offsetting deterioration in the value of the written credit derivative, either through reductions in fair value or by an addition to reserves, reflected in the bank's tier 1 capital, the credit protection shall not be recognised for the purpose of offsetting the effective notional amounts related to written credit derivative instruments.` | Where credit protection is purchased via a total return swap but offsetting deterioration in written credit derivative value is not reflected in Tier 1 capital (as fair value reduction or reserves addition), not recognise the TRS protection as an offset against written credit derivative notional in the leverage ratio exposure measure. | Capital Management Policy; Market Risk Policy; Hedging Policy | Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance); Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | entity-only | otc-ird | ACT-RISK-CAPITAL | riskTaxonomy: RT-CR.CP | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-SACCR-010 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d7-2020-saccr-implementation-date:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D7/2020** §2.7 — Implementation date. `§2.7: Banks must comply with this directive simultaneously with the implementation of the amended Regulations that incorporate the SA-CCR methodology.` + D7/2020 §3.1 (Acknowledgement of Receipt obligation): `§3.1: Kindly ensure that a copy of this Directive is made available to your institution's external auditors. The attached acknowledgment of receipt duly completed and signed by both the chief executive officer of the institution and the said auditors should be returned to the PA at the earliest convenience.` Issued by: Kuben Naidoo, Deputy Governor and CEO: Prudential Authority, Date: 2020-12-01. | Comply with Directive D7/2020 (SA-CCR leverage ratio methodology) simultaneously with the implementation of the amended Regulations incorporating SA-CCR; provide the directive to external auditors; return the completed acknowledgement of receipt signed by the CEO and auditors to the PA at the earliest convenience. | Regulatory Engagement Policy; Capital Management Policy | Owen (Company Secretary, governance) — acknowledgement-of-receipt custody; Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance) — CEO signatory + SA-CCR implementation gate | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | entity-only | otc-ird | fx | ACT-RISK-CAPITAL, ACT-GOVERN-COMPLIANCE | riskTaxonomy: RT-LR.RC | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

## Domain A-PVA — Prudent Valuation Adjustments (D5/2020)

> *Added 2026-05-28 (v1.34) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) via structured-JSON extraction of `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d5-2020-structured.json`. D5/2020 is issued by the SARB Prudential Authority under Banks Act s.6(6) to establish the PVA framework for prudent valuation of fair-valued positions. Effective date: 2020-09-02 (issued by Kuben Naidoo; previous directive was D4/2020 dated 27 August 2020). PVA obligations apply to the bank at solo, consolidated, controlling-company-consolidated, branch and subsidiary levels. All rows: `Bind-trigger: commencement` (fair-valued trading positions arise at commencement of trading), `Entity scope: bank`, `review-status: new-from-llm-extraction`. The obligations are extracted from the directive body (§§2–5) and Annexure A methodology.*

| ID | URN | Citation | Requirement | Fulfilment policy | Owner | Status | Bind-trigger | Entity scope | Applies-at | Product scope | Activity scope | Risk taxonomy | review-status | review-author | review-date | review-event-id |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ORG-PR-PVA-001 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d5-2020-pva-individual-consideration:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D5/2020** §2.1 — Individual PVA consideration obligation. `§2.1: Banks have to consider all PVAs individually (that is, regardless of the ability to associate them with a single PVA group or category). However, banks may estimate PVAs in line with internal governance models. Banks must, at a minimum, consider the following PVAs: [§2.1.1 Investing and funding costs; §2.1.2 Close-out uncertainty (mid-market value, close-out cost, concentration); §2.1.3 Unearned credit spreads; §2.1.4 Early termination; §2.1.5 Model risk; §2.1.6 Operational risk; §2.1.7 Future administrative costs; §2.1.8 Other; §2.1.9 DVA excluded].` Reg 39(13)(c)(i)(D) (PVA categories in the Regulations). | Consider all PVAs individually for all fair-valued positions at a minimum across the eight prescribed categories: investing and funding costs; close-out uncertainty (mid-market value, close-out cost, concentration); unearned credit spreads; early termination; model risk; operational risk; future administrative costs; and other; noting that Debit Valuation Adjustments (DVA) are excluded from individual PVAs. | Valuation Policy; Market Risk Policy; Capital Management Policy | Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — PVA engine; Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance) — CET1 capital impact; Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — model risk governance | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | otc-ird | listed-bond | listed-equity | repo | ACT-RISK-MARKET, ACT-RISK-MODEL, ACT-RISK-CAPITAL | riskTaxonomy: RT-MK | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-PVA-002 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d5-2020-pva-materiality-threshold:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D5/2020** Annexure A §2.1.1.1 — Materiality threshold for simplified vs core approach. `§2.1.1.1: The simplified approach can only be adopted where the total adjustments to fair values that affect CET 1 as a percentage of the sum of the absolute value of fair-valued assets and liabilities (excluding DVA) is below 1%. For fair-valued assets and liabilities whose adjustments to fair values has a partial or zero impact on CET 1 capital, their values must only be included to the extent of the impact on CET 1. Exactly matching, offsetting fair valued assets and liabilities are excluded. §2.1.1.3: This materiality threshold must be applied at the following levels: bank solo, bank consolidated, controlling company consolidated, foreign branch and foreign subsidiary. Where the threshold is breached at controlling company level, the core approach must be applied at all levels in the banking group.` | Apply the materiality threshold test to determine whether the simplified or core approach applies for PVA calculation: if total fair-value adjustments affecting CET1, as a percentage of absolute fair-valued assets and liabilities (excluding DVA), is below 1%, the simplified approach is permitted; if the threshold is breached at any level — including controlling-company consolidated — the core approach must be applied at all levels in the banking group. | Valuation Policy; Capital Management Policy | Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance) — CET1 threshold calculation; Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — PVA engine | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | otc-ird | listed-bond | listed-equity | repo | ACT-RISK-CAPITAL, ACT-RISK-MARKET | riskTaxonomy: RT-MK | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-PVA-003 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d5-2020-pva-simplified-approach:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D5/2020** Annexure A §3.1–§3.3 — Simplified approach calculation. `§3.1: Banks must calculate the PVA under the simplified approach as 0.1% of the sum of absolute value of fair-valued assets and liabilities which are included in the materiality threshold calculation in paragraph 2.1.1 above. §3.2: the aggregate PVA must be the PVA resulting from the calculation in §3.1. §3.3: for Pillar 3 disclosure requirements and Template PV1, the aggregate PVA from the calculation in §3.1 must be allocated to individual PVA categories.` + D5/2020 §2.3: follow Annexure A requirements for individual PVA determination. | Where the simplified approach applies (materiality threshold < 1%), calculate the aggregate PVA as 0.1% of the sum of the absolute value of fair-valued assets and liabilities; allocate the aggregate PVA to individual PVA categories for Pillar 3 Template PV1 disclosure. | Valuation Policy; Pillar 3 Disclosure Policy | Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — simplified PVA computation; Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance) — Pillar 3 PV1 disclosure | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | otc-ird | listed-bond | listed-equity | repo | ACT-RISK-CAPITAL, ACT-REPORT-PRUDENTIAL | riskTaxonomy: RT-MK | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-PVA-004 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d5-2020-pva-core-approach-overview:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D5/2020** Annexure A §4.1.1–§4.2.4 — Core approach — general provisions. `§4.1.1: Banks must calculate PVAs under the core approach by: (a) calculating PVAs for each category described in Reg 39(13)(c)(i)(D); (b) summing category-level PVAs to provide the aggregate PVA. §4.2.1: For fair-valued assets and liabilities where accounting valuation change has partial or zero impact on CET1 capital, PVAs must only be calculated based on the proportion impacting CET1 (excludes DVA already deducted). §4.2.3: PVAs must be excess of valuation adjustments required to achieve prudent value over adjustments applied in fair value addressing the same source of valuation uncertainty. §4.2.4: PVAs must always be positive, including at valuation exposure level, category level, both pre- and post-aggregation.` Reg 39(13)(c)(i)(D) (PVA categories). | Under the core approach, calculate PVAs for each prescribed PVA category per Reg 39(13)(c)(i)(D), then sum category-level PVAs to the aggregate; ensure PVAs are always positive at valuation exposure level and category level; calculate only the proportion of PVA impacting CET1 for assets where accounting fair-value change has partial or zero CET1 impact. | Valuation Policy; Capital Management Policy | Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — core PVA engine; Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance) — CET1 impact scoping | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | otc-ird | listed-bond | listed-equity | repo | ACT-RISK-CAPITAL, ACT-RISK-MARKET | riskTaxonomy: RT-MK | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-PVA-005 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d5-2020-pva-mid-market-value:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D5/2020** Annexure A §4.3.1–§4.3.6 — Mid-market value PVA calculation. `§4.3.1: Mid-market value PVAs must be calculated at valuation exposure level. §4.3.2: The mid-market value PVA must only be assessed to have zero value where: (a) the bank has firm evidence of a tradable price for a valuation exposure or reliable data based on a liquid market; and (b) market data sources do not indicate any material valuation uncertainty. §4.3.3: Where a valuation exposure cannot be shown to have zero PVA, banks must use market data sources when assessing the mid-market value PVA. §4.3.6: Banks must calculate the total category level PVA for mid-market value by applying to individual mid-market value PVAs the formulae for either Method 1 or Method 2 per Exhibit 1.` | Calculate mid-market value PVAs at valuation exposure level for all fair-valued positions; assess zero mid-market value PVA only where both conditions are met (firm tradable price evidence and no material valuation uncertainty); aggregate using Method 1 or Method 2 formulae from Exhibit 1 of Annexure A. | Valuation Policy; Independent Price Verification Procedure | Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — mid-market PVA; Anya (Data-as-code engineer, engineering) — market data sourcing | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | otc-ird | listed-bond | listed-equity | repo | ACT-RISK-MARKET, ACT-RISK-MODEL | riskTaxonomy: RT-MK | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-PVA-006 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d5-2020-pva-close-out-cost:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D5/2020** Annexure A §4.4.1–§4.4.7 — Close-out cost PVA calculation. `§4.4.1: Close-out cost PVAs must be calculated at valuation exposure level. §4.4.3: The close-out cost PVA may be assessed to have zero value on condition that the bank provides evidence that it is 90% confident that sufficient liquidity exists to support the exit of the related valuation exposures at mid-price. §4.4.7: Banks must calculate the total category level PVA for close-out cost by applying to the individual close-out cost PVAs the formulae for either Method 1 or Method 2 per Exhibit 1.` | Calculate close-out cost PVAs at valuation exposure level; assess zero close-out cost PVA only where the bank has 90% confidence that sufficient liquidity exists to support exit at mid-price; aggregate using Method 1 or Method 2 from Exhibit 1 of Annexure A. | Valuation Policy; Liquidity Risk Management Policy | Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering); Eitan (Treasurer, governance) — exit-liquidity assessment | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | otc-ird | listed-bond | listed-equity | repo | ACT-RISK-MARKET, ACT-RISK-LIQUIDITY | riskTaxonomy: RT-MK | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-PVA-007 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d5-2020-pva-model-risk:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D5/2020** Annexure A §4.5.1–§4.5.6 — Model risk PVA calculation. `§4.5.1: Where appropriate, banks must estimate a model risk PVA for each valuation model by considering valuation model risk which arises due to the potential existence of a range of different models or model calibrations, which are used by market participants, and the lack of a firm exit price for the specific product being valued. Banks must not consider valuation model risk which arises due to calibrations from market derived parameters (must be captured in mid-market value PVA). §4.5.5: Where banks use a specific method, the prudence must be confirmed annually by comparison to an alternative approach on a material sample of valuation models. §4.5.6: Banks must calculate the total category level PVA for model risk by applying to individual model risk PVAs the formulae for either Method 1 or Method 2 per Exhibit 1.` | Estimate a model risk PVA for each valuation model covering: range of models/calibrations used by market participants; lack of firm exit price; not including market-derived-parameter risk (captured in mid-market PVA); confirm the prudence of the chosen method annually for a material sample; aggregate using Method 1 or Method 2 from Exhibit 1. | Valuation Policy; Model Risk Policy | Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — model risk PVA engine; Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — model risk governance + annual prudence confirmation | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | otc-ird | listed-bond | listed-equity | repo | ACT-RISK-MODEL, ACT-RISK-MARKET | riskTaxonomy: RT-MK, RT-OP.MD | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-PVA-008 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d5-2020-pva-unearned-credit-spreads:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D5/2020** Annexure A §4.6.1–§4.6.2 — Unearned credit spreads PVA. `§4.6.1: Banks must calculate the unearned credit spreads PVA to reflect the valuation uncertainty in the adjustment necessary, in accordance with the applicable accounting framework, to include the present value of expected losses due to counterparty default on derivative positions, discounted using a rate which approximate the risk-free rate. This PVA must be considered at counterparty level. §4.6.2: Banks must include the element of the PVA relating to mid-market value within the mid-market value PVA category; the element relating to close-out cost uncertainty within the close-out costs PVA category; the element relating to model risk within the model risk PVA category.` | Calculate the unearned credit spreads PVA at counterparty level to reflect valuation uncertainty in CVA (present value of expected losses from counterparty default, discounted at risk-free rate); decompose the unearned credit spreads PVA elements across mid-market value, close-out cost, and model risk PVA categories. | Valuation Policy; Credit Risk Policy; Capital Management Policy | Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — CVA/unearned-credit-spreads PVA; Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — counterparty credit risk oversight | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | otc-ird | fx | ACT-RISK-CREDIT, ACT-RISK-MARKET | riskTaxonomy: RT-CR.CP, RT-MK | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-PVA-009 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d5-2020-pva-concentration:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D5/2020** Annexure A §4.8.1–§4.8.3 — Concentration PVA. `§4.8.1: Banks must estimate a concentration PVA for concentrated valuation positions by applying the following three-step approach: (a) identify concentrated valuation positions; (b) for each identified concentrated valuation position where market price for size is unavailable, estimate a prudent exit period; (c) only where the prudent exit period exceeds 10 days, estimate a PVA taking into account volatility of the valuation input, volatility of the bid-offer spread, and impact of hypothetical exit strategy on market prices. §4.8.2: Identification must consider: size of positions relative to market liquidity; the institution's ability to trade in that market; average daily market volume and typical daily trading volume of the bank. §4.8.3: Banks must establish and document the methodology for determining concentrated positions.` | Estimate a concentration PVA for concentrated valuation positions using the three-step approach (identification, prudent exit period estimation, PVA calculation for positions with exit period > 10 days); document the concentration-identification methodology considering relative position size, market liquidity, and trading volumes. | Valuation Policy; Market Risk Policy; Independent Price Verification Procedure | Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — concentration PVA engine + documented methodology; Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — model risk governance | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | otc-ird | listed-bond | listed-equity | repo | ACT-RISK-MARKET, ACT-RISK-LIQUIDITY | riskTaxonomy: RT-MK, RT-LQ.FN | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-PVA-010 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d5-2020-pva-future-admin-costs:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D5/2020** Annexure A §4.9.1–§4.9.4 — Future administrative costs PVA. `§4.9.2: Where a valuation exposure cannot be shown to have zero PVA per §4.9.1, banks must calculate the future administrative cost PVA considering administrative costs and future hedging costs over the expected life of valuation exposures for which a direct exit price is not applied for close-out costs, discounted at a rate approximating the risk-free rate. §4.9.3: Administrative costs must include all incremental staffing and fixed costs that will be incurred in managing the portfolio. §4.9.4: Banks must calculate the total category level PVA for future administrative costs as the sum of individual future administrative costs PVAs.` | Calculate future administrative cost PVAs for valuation exposures where close-out cost PVA is non-zero, covering administrative and future hedging costs over the expected portfolio life (discounted at risk-free rate), including all incremental staffing and fixed costs; sum individual future administrative cost PVAs to the category-level aggregate. | Valuation Policy; Capital Management Policy | Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering); Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance) — incremental cost identification | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | otc-ird | listed-bond | fx | ACT-RISK-MARKET, ACT-RISK-CAPITAL | riskTaxonomy: RT-MK, RT-OP | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-PVA-011 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d5-2020-pva-operational-risk-ipv:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D5/2020** Annexure A §4.11.1 — Operational risk PVA and IPV process requirement. `§4.11.1: Banks must estimate a PVA for operational risk to be zero, provided that the bank has a defined IPV process which is audited both internally and externally, and the audit outcome of the IPV process has indicated no material process failure. Where the bank does not have a defined IPV process which is audited both internally and externally or the audit outcome has indicated a material process failure, the bank must calculate an operational risk PVA as 10% of the sum of the aggregated category level PVAs for mid-market value and close-out costs.` | Maintain a defined independent price verification (IPV) process that is audited both internally and externally with no material process failure, in order to assess zero operational risk PVA; where no such IPV process exists or where a material process failure is found by audit, calculate the operational risk PVA as 10% of the sum of aggregated category-level PVAs for mid-market value and close-out costs. | Valuation Policy; Internal Audit Policy; Independent Price Verification Procedure | Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — IPV process and operational risk PVA; Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — audit oversight; Vera (Internal audit engineer, audit) — IPV process audit | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | otc-ird | listed-bond | listed-equity | repo | ACT-RISK-OPERATIONAL, ACT-RISK-MARKET | riskTaxonomy: RT-OP.PR, RT-MK | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-PVA-012 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d5-2020-pva-reporting-disclosure:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D5/2020** §4.1–§4.3 (Reporting and disclosure obligations, directive body). `§4.1: The aggregate PVA must be reported in line item 203 of the form BA 700. §4.2: Banks must estimate their PVAs at least on a quarterly basis. The PVA must be disclosed annually, in accordance with Directive 1 of 2019. Rows which are not applicable to the reporting bank must be completed as zero ('0') and the reason why they are not applicable must be explained in the accompanying narrative. §4.3: The PVAs must be estimated at bank solo, bank consolidated, controlling company consolidated and branches as well as subsidiaries of local banks. The rationale behind estimating at consolidated level is to minimise any arbitrage of the framework through the use of complex organisation structures, which could be cross jurisdictional.` **substrate-note (BA 700 line-number mapping):** D5/2020 §4.1 maps the aggregate PVA to **BA 700 line item 203**. The BA 700 renderer should resolve `BA700:line_203` to this obligation. | Report the aggregate PVA in BA 700 line item 203; estimate PVAs at least quarterly; disclose PVAs annually in accordance with Directive 1 of 2019 (Pillar 3); complete non-applicable rows with zero and explanatory narrative; estimate PVAs at bank solo, bank consolidated, controlling-company consolidated, and branch/subsidiary levels to prevent cross-jurisdictional arbitrage. | Valuation Policy; Regulatory Reporting Policy; Pillar 3 Disclosure Policy; Capital Management Policy | Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance) — BA 700 submission + annual Pillar 3 PVA disclosure; Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — quarterly PVA estimation engine; Bea (Accounting & financial reporting engineer, engineering) — BA 700 line 203 population | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | otc-ird | listed-bond | listed-equity | repo | ACT-RISK-CAPITAL, ACT-REPORT-PRUDENTIAL | riskTaxonomy: RT-MK, RT-LR.RC | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

## Domain A-OR-RESILIENCE — Operational Resilience (D4/2023)

> *Added 2026-05-28 (v1.35) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) via structured-JSON extraction of `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d4-2023-structured.json`. D4/2023 is issued by the SARB Prudential Authority under Banks Act s.6(6)(a) and replaces Directive 10 of 2021. It applies to all banks, branches of foreign institutions, and controlling companies. The directive implements the BCBS Principles for Operational Resilience (March 2021, BIS/BCBS/publ/d516) across seven categories: governance; operational risk management; business continuity planning and testing; mapping of interconnections and interdependencies; third-party dependency management; incident management; and resilient ICT including cyber security. Compliance deadline: 31 December 2024 per §2.2. The operational resilience framework must be in place before licence. **Bind-trigger:** `LICENCE-BIND` (operational resilience framework must be in place before licence is granted). All rows: `Entity scope: bank`, `Applies-at: consolidated` (§1.3 states the principles are required to be applied on a consolidated basis), `review-status: new-from-llm-extraction`.*

| ID | URN | Citation | Requirement | Fulfilment policy | Owner | Status | Bind-trigger | Entity scope | Applies-at | Product scope | Activity scope | Risk taxonomy | review-status | review-author | review-date | review-event-id |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ORG-OR-RESILIENCE-001 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d4-2023-or-assess-adequacy:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D4/2023** §2.1.1 + §1.3. `§2.1.1: Banks must assess the adequacy and robustness of their current policies, processes and practices against the principles for operational resilience issued by the BCBS. §1.3: The principles are required to be applied on a consolidated basis and also form an integral part of a bank's forward-looking operational resilience approach in line with its operational risk appetite and tolerance for disruption.` BCBS Principles for Operational Resilience (March 2021, https://www.bis.org/bcbs/publ/d516.htm). Enabling authority: Banks Act 94/1990 s.6(6)(a); Reg 39 (operational risk management process). | Assess the adequacy and robustness of current policies, processes and practices against the BCBS Principles for Operational Resilience (March 2021), applying the assessment on a consolidated basis and integrating it with the bank's forward-looking operational resilience approach in line with its risk appetite and tolerance for disruption. | Operational Resilience Policy; Risk Management Framework; `Procedures/by-policy/operational-resilience.md` (planned) | Devon (Chief Operating Officer, governance) — primary; Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — risk appetite and tolerance dimension | NOT_APPLICABLE | licence-day | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-RISK-OPERATIONAL | riskTaxonomy: RT-OP.RE | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

## Domain A-PR-RETURNS — BA Returns to the Prudential Authority (D5/2025)

> *Added 2026-05-28 (v1.35) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) via structured-JSON extraction of `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d5-2025-structured.json`. D5/2025 is issued by the SARB Prudential Authority under Banks Act s.6(6)(a) and replaces Directive 4 of 2024 and Directive 1 of 2025 with effect from 1 July 2025 (§3.1). It consolidates all BA return obligations — formerly embedded in the Regulations Relating to Banks — into a single directive following the Umoja System Implementation Project (§1.2). D5/2025 directs banks to complete and submit 28 BA forms. Implementation date: returns BA 100–BA 700 effective from 1 February 2025 (§3.2.1); returns BA 900–BA 940 and SARB Economic Statistics returns effective from 1 February 2025 (§3.2.2). Accompanying certification: every return set must be accompanied by a duly signed BA 099 (bank/controlling company) or BA 099A (foreign operation). **Bind-trigger:** `COMMENCEMENT-BIND` (BA returns reflect trading activity; first submission obligation arises once the bank commences trading). All rows: `Entity scope: bank`, `review-status: new-from-llm-extraction`. **Note on existing row `ORG-PR-29`:** v1.14 added `ORG-PR-29` as a high-level placeholder for D2/2024 BA returns under Reg 46. D5/2025 supersedes D4/2024 and D1/2025 and is the current consolidated BA returns directive. The `ORG-PR-29` placeholder is retained; the rows below provide granular per-form coverage under D5/2025.*

| ID | URN | Citation | Requirement | Fulfilment policy | Owner | Status | Bind-trigger | Entity scope | Applies-at | Product scope | Activity scope | Risk taxonomy | review-status | review-author | review-date | review-event-id |

| ORG-PR-RETURNS-014 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d5-2025-returns-ba330-large-exposures:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D5/2025** §2.1.15. `§2.1.15: Complete form BA 330, attached to this Directive as Annexure 14A, in accordance with the requirements specified in Directive 2 of 2023, read with the relevant requirements specified in the Regulations.` Directive 2 of 2023; `ORG-PR-09`; `ORG-PR-40` (D3/2022 LEX directive). `[correction (Mira, v1.39, WS-INSTRUMENT-ANALYSES, verified against public source): the prior annotation "BA 330 is the large exposures return" and "Reg 25 (large exposure limits)" are BOTH incorrect. (1) Form BA 330 is the INTEREST-RATE-RISK-IN-THE-BANKING-BOOK (IRRBB) repricing-gap return — confirmed by SARB PA Directive 2 of 2023 (the very directive D5/2025 §2.1.15 above references), issued "in terms of regulation 30 of the Regulations relating to Banks", whose §1.2–1.3 and column/line-item tables define BA 330 as the IRRBB repricing-gap return (gazetted 23 Dec 2022, effective 1 Jan 2023). The large-exposures regime is reported within the credit-risk return family (BA 200-series), not on BA 330. (2) The LEX-implementing regulation is Reg 24(6)–(8), NOT Reg 25 (per BCBS RCAP April 2023 Annex 2). The verbatim D5/2025 §2.1.15 quote above is internally consistent with BA 330 = IRRBB (it routes BA 330 to Directive 2 of 2023). The precise large-exposures return form number + cadence remain counsel-gated (Imani + external counsel at licence gate). ## Domain RM-RDARR — Effective Risk Data Aggregation and Risk Reporting (D2/2015 / BCBS 239)

> *Added 2026-05-28 (v1.35) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) via structured-JSON extraction of `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d2-2015-structured.json`. D2/2015 is issued by the SARB Prudential Authority under Banks Act s.6(6)(a). It applies to all banks, branches of foreign institutions, and controlling companies. The directive mandates effective risk data aggregation and risk reporting — the SA implementation of BCBS 239 (January 2013: "Principles for Effective Risk Data Aggregation and Risk Reporting"). The directive remains effective as confirmed in Banks Act Circular 1 of the relevant year (§1.3). The structured JSON source is sparse (3 subsections: introduction, application, acknowledgement); the BCBS 239 obligations are derived from the directive's express incorporation of the BCBS 239 principles via §1.2 (titled "Effective risk data aggregation and risk reporting") and §2.2 (institutions required to ensure compliance). Fourteen BCBS 239 principles apply across two groups: (A) Risk data aggregation capabilities (Principles 1–6) and (B) Risk reporting practices (Principles 7–11), plus governance and infrastructure overarch (Principles 12–14). **Bind-trigger:** `COMMENCEMENT-BIND` (risk data aggregation and risk reporting obligations arise once the bank commences trading and generates risk data). All rows: `Entity scope: bank`, `review-status: new-from-llm-extraction`.*

| ID | URN | Citation | Requirement | Fulfilment policy | Owner | Status | Bind-trigger | Entity scope | Applies-at | Product scope | Activity scope | Risk taxonomy | review-status | review-author | review-date | review-event-id |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ORG-RM-RDARR-001 | `urn:obligation:bank:rdarr:pa-d2-2015-governance-infrastructure:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D2/2015** §1.1–§1.2 + §2.1–§2.2 — Governance and infrastructure overarch (BCBS 239 Principles 1–2). `§1.2: Effective risk data aggregation and risk reporting. §2.2: Institutions are required to ensure compliance with the requirements set out herein.` BCBS 239 Principle 1 (Governance): A bank should establish a strong governance framework for risk data aggregation capabilities and risk reporting practices, covering approval of the data aggregation and reporting framework by the board; senior management accountability; written policies and procedures; and data quality controls. BCBS 239 Principle 2 (Data Architecture and IT Infrastructure): A bank should design, build and maintain data architecture and IT infrastructure which fully supports its risk data aggregation capabilities and risk reporting practices not only in normal times but also in times of stress or crisis. Enabling authority: Banks Act 94/1990 s.6(6)(a); Reg 39 (corporate governance and risk management). | Establish a strong governance framework for risk data aggregation and risk reporting as required by BCBS 239 Principle 1, including board approval of the RDARR framework, senior management accountability, written policies and procedures, and data quality controls; and design, build, and maintain data architecture and IT infrastructure that fully supports risk data aggregation and risk reporting in both normal and stressed conditions (BCBS 239 Principle 2). | Risk Data Aggregation and Reporting Policy; Data Governance Policy; Operational Risk Policy | Devon (Chief Operating Officer, governance) — IT infrastructure; Anya (Data-as-code engineer, engineering) — data architecture; Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — risk reporting governance | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-RISK-OPERATIONAL, ACT-TECH-DATA | riskTaxonomy: RT-OP, RT-OP.RE | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

BCBS 239 Principle 3 (Accuracy and Integrity): A bank should be able to generate accurate and reliable risk data to meet normal and stress/crisis reporting accuracy requirements. Data should be aggregated on a largely automated basis so as to minimise the probability of errors. A single authoritative source of risk data should exist for each type of risk. | Aggregate risk data so as to be able to generate accurate and reliable risk data to meet normal and stress/crisis reporting accuracy requirements; base aggregation largely on automated processes to minimise error probability; maintain a single authoritative source of risk data for each risk type. | Risk Data Aggregation and Reporting Policy; Data Quality Policy | Anya (Data-as-code engineer, engineering) — data pipeline and single-source; Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — accuracy standards | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-RISK-OPERATIONAL, ACT-TECH-DATA | riskTaxonomy: RT-OP, RT-OP.RE | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

BCBS 239 Principle 4 (Completeness): A bank should be able to capture and aggregate all material risk data across the banking group; data should be available by business line, legal entity, asset type, industry, region, and other groupings as relevant for the risk in question; this allows identification and reporting of risk exposures, concentrations and emerging risks. BCBS 239 Principle 5 (Timeliness): A bank should be able to generate aggregate and up-to-date risk data in a timely manner while also meeting the principles relating to accuracy and integrity; the precise timing will depend on the nature and potential volatility of the risk being measured as well as its criticality to the overall risk profile of the bank. | Capture and aggregate all material risk data across the banking group with completeness across business lines, legal entities, asset types, industries, and regions to enable risk exposure and concentration identification (BCBS 239 Principle 4); generate aggregate and up-to-date risk data in a timely manner, with timeliness requirements calibrated to the nature and volatility of each risk type (BCBS 239 Principle 5). | Risk Data Aggregation and Reporting Policy; Data Governance Policy; Risk Management Framework | Anya (Data-as-code engineer, engineering) — data pipeline completeness; Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — risk data timeliness standards; Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — risk metrics timeliness | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-RISK-OPERATIONAL, ACT-TECH-DATA | riskTaxonomy: RT-OP, RT-OP.RE | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

BCBS 239 Principle 6 (Adaptability): A bank should be able to generate aggregate risk data to meet a broad range of on-demand, ad hoc risk reporting requests, including requests during stress/crisis situations; a bank should be able to generate aggregate risk data for new risk types or for new business areas and produce sub-consolidated or individual entity risk data. | Generate aggregate risk data on demand and in ad hoc scenarios — including during stress or crisis situations — covering new risk types, new business areas, and sub-consolidated or individual entity-level data (BCBS 239 Principle 6). | Risk Data Aggregation and Reporting Policy; Stress Testing Policy | Anya (Data-as-code engineer, engineering) — event-store query adaptability; Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — on-demand reporting standards | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-RISK-OPERATIONAL, ACT-TECH-DATA | riskTaxonomy: RT-OP, RT-OP.RE | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

BCBS 239 Principle 7 (Accuracy): Risk management reports should accurately and precisely convey aggregated risk data and reflect risk in an exact manner; reports should be reconciled and validated; significant data gaps should be identified and explained; any manual intervention in the reporting process should be clearly documented. | Produce risk management reports that accurately and precisely convey aggregated risk data, are reconciled and validated, identify and explain any significant data gaps, and document any manual interventions in the reporting process (BCBS 239 Principle 7). | Risk Data Aggregation and Reporting Policy; Regulatory Reporting Policy | Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — risk reporting accuracy; Anya (Data-as-code engineer, engineering) — report reconciliation automation | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-RISK-OPERATIONAL, ACT-REPORT-PRUDENTIAL | riskTaxonomy: RT-OP, RT-LR.RC | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

BCBS 239 Principle 8 (Comprehensiveness): Risk management reports should cover all material risk areas within the organisation; the depth and scope of these reports should be consistent with the size and complexity of the bank's operations and risk profile, and the requirements of the recipients; forward-looking assessments of risk should be provided. BCBS 239 Principle 9 (Clarity and Usefulness): Risk management reports should communicate information in a clear and concise manner; reports should be easy to understand yet comprehensive enough to allow recipients to make informed decisions; reports should include information on both absolute and relative levels of risk. | Produce risk management reports that cover all material risk areas with depth and scope calibrated to the bank's size, complexity, and recipients' requirements, including forward-looking assessments (BCBS 239 Principle 8); and present information clearly and concisely to enable informed decisions, covering both absolute and relative risk levels (BCBS 239 Principle 9). | Risk Data Aggregation and Reporting Policy; Risk Management Framework | Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — risk reporting standards; Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance) — financial risk report comprehensiveness | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-RISK-OPERATIONAL, ACT-REPORT-PRUDENTIAL | riskTaxonomy: RT-OP, RT-LR.RC | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

BCBS 239 Principle 10 (Frequency): The board and senior management should set the frequency of risk management report production and distribution; the frequency of reports should be increased during times of stress/crisis; reports for the board and senior management should be produced at least monthly. BCBS 239 Principle 11 (Distribution): Risk management reports should be distributed to the relevant parties while ensuring that the information remains confidential. | Set the frequency of risk management report production and distribution, with at least monthly reporting to the board and senior management; increase reporting frequency during stress or crisis conditions (BCBS 239 Principle 10); distribute risk reports to relevant recipients while maintaining confidentiality of the information (BCBS 239 Principle 11). | Risk Data Aggregation and Reporting Policy; Information Security Policy | Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — reporting cadence; Owen (Company Secretary, governance) — board reporting schedule; CISO (when activated) — confidentiality of distributed reports | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-RISK-OPERATIONAL, ACT-REPORT-PRUDENTIAL | riskTaxonomy: RT-OP, RT-LR.RC | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-RM-RDARR-008 | `urn:obligation:bank:rdarr:pa-d2-2015-supervisory-review:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D2/2015** §2.1 (Application scope) + §2.2 (compliance obligation) — Supervisory review and remediation (BCBS 239 Principles 12–14). BCBS 239 Principle 12 (Review): Supervisors should periodically review and evaluate a bank's compliance with the eleven principles above; supervisors should make use of multiple evaluative techniques — using a range of supervisory tools — to conduct such evaluations. BCBS 239 Principle 13 (Remedial Actions and Supervisory Measures): Supervisors should have and use the appropriate tools and resources to require effective and timely remedial action by a bank to address deficiencies in its risk data aggregation capabilities and risk reporting practices; supervisors should provide appropriate capacity to execute such oversight. BCBS 239 Principle 14 (Home/Host Cooperation): Home and host supervisors of internationally active banks should cooperate with each other and regularly share relevant supervisory information on the risk data aggregation and risk reporting practices of the banks they oversee, in line with the information-sharing principles in the Basel Committee's Core Principles for Effective Banking Supervision. `§2.1: This directive applies to all banks, branches of foreign institutions and controlling companies registered or operating in South Africa. §2.2: Institutions are required to ensure compliance with the requirements set out herein.` | Comply with all BCBS 239 principles (1–11) as required by D2/2015, including readiness for supervisory review under Principles 12–14: maintain documented risk data aggregation and reporting frameworks sufficient to support PA supervisory evaluation; remediate any deficiencies identified by supervisory review in a timely manner; and ensure cross-border cooperation readiness under the home/host cooperation principle where applicable. | Risk Data Aggregation and Reporting Policy; Regulatory Engagement Policy; `Procedures/by-policy/ba-returns-regulation-46.md` (planned) | Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — RDARR compliance programme lead; Iris (Regulator-relations engineer, engineering) — PA supervisory engagement; Anya (Data-as-code engineer, engineering) — data infrastructure compliance evidence | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-RISK-OPERATIONAL, ACT-GOVERN-COMPLIANCE | riskTaxonomy: RT-OP, RT-LR.RC | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

## Domain PR-P3 — Pillar 3 Disclosure: Interest Rate Risk in the Banking Book (D1/2024)

> *Added 2026-05-28 (v1.36) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) via structured-JSON extraction of `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d1-2024-structured.json`. D1/2024 is issued by the SARB Prudential Authority under Banks Act s.6(6)(a), effective 1 January 2024. It applies to all banks, branches of foreign institutions, and controlling companies registered or operating in South Africa. The directive mandates Pillar 3 disclosure requirements for interest rate risk in the banking book (IRRBB), remaining effective as confirmed in Banks Act Circular 1 of the relevant year (§1.3). The directive supplements the general Pillar 3 disclosure framework (D10/2025 per `ORG-PR-28`) with IRRBB-specific disclosure obligations, consistent with the BCBS IRRBB standards. **Bind-trigger:** `COMMENCEMENT-BIND` (IRRBB Pillar 3 disclosure obligations arise when the bank has banking book interest rate exposures at commencement of trading; effective date 1 January 2024 per D1/2024 §1.3 / effective date context). **Note on existing rows:** `ORG-PR-28` (D10/2025 Pillar 3 general disclosure) and `ORG-PR-RETURNS-018` (BA 410 return) remain; the new `ORG-PR-P3-001..003` rows supplement with IRRBB-specific Pillar 3 obligations from D1/2024. All rows: `Entity scope: bank`, `review-status: new-from-llm-extraction`.*

## Domain PR-LCR — Liquidity Coverage Ratio: Scope of Application and Calculation (D1/2022)

> *Added 2026-05-28 (v1.36) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) via structured-JSON extraction of `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d1-2022-structured.json`. D1/2022 is issued by the SARB Prudential Authority under Banks Act s.6(6)(a), effective 11 February 2022 (signed by Kuben Naidoo, Deputy Governor and CEO: Prudential Authority, Date: 2022-02-11). It replaces Directive 11 of 2014 (§1.3). The directive applies to all banks, branches of foreign institutions, and controlling companies. It provides the scope of application for the LCR as implemented in the Regulations Relating to Banks (GG No. 35950, 10 April 2015), specifying consolidation methodology, currency treatment, intragroup elimination, and public disclosure requirements. The LCR promotes short-term resilience of banks to liquidity risk by ensuring sufficient unencumbered HQLA to meet liquidity needs for a 30 calendar-day stress scenario (§1.2). **Bind-trigger:** `COMMENCEMENT-BIND` (LCR compliance and reporting obligations arise at commencement of trading; the bank must maintain a continuous LCR from the first day of operations). **Note on existing rows:** `ORG-PR-06` (v1.0 high-level LCR obligation under Reg 26) and `ORG-PR-RETURNS-003` (BA 110 LCR return) remain; the new `ORG-PR-LCR-001..010` rows are granular supplements extracted from D1/2022 verbatim directive text. All rows: `Entity scope: bank`, `review-status: new-from-llm-extraction`.*

| ID | URN | Citation | Requirement | Fulfilment policy | Owner | Status | Bind-trigger | Entity scope | Applies-at | Product scope | Activity scope | Risk taxonomy | review-status | review-author | review-date | review-event-id |

| ORG-PR-LCR-001 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d1-2022-lcr-hqla-maintenance:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D1/2022** §1.2 + §2.3. `§1.2: The LCR promotes short-term resilience of banks' exposure to liquidity risk by ensuring that they have sufficient unencumbered high-quality liquid assets (HQLA) that can be easily and immediately converted into cash in private markets to meet their liquidity needs for a 30 calendar-day liquidity stress scenario. §2.3: Consolidation within this context should be read in conjunction with the objective of the LCR, which is to ensure that every bank maintains an adequate stock of HQLA to meet its liquidity needs for a 30 calendar-day liquidity stress scenario.` Regulations Relating to Banks Reg 26(12). | Maintain an adequate stock of unencumbered HQLA sufficient to meet the bank's liquidity needs for a 30 calendar-day liquidity stress scenario, both at solo and consolidated levels. | Liquidity Risk Management Policy; LCR Policy; HQLA Management Policy | Eitan (Treasurer, governance) — HQLA portfolio management; Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — liquidity risk appetite; Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — LCR computation | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-RISK-LIQUIDITY | riskTaxonomy: RT-LQ.FN | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

| ORG-PR-LCR-003 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d1-2022-lcr-consolidated-scope:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D1/2022** §4.1.2 + §4.1.2.1–§4.1.2.4. `§4.1.2.1: only banking and/or deposit-taking entities shall be included; §4.1.2.2: branches shall be consolidated in accordance with the requirements specified in paragraph 2.5 above; §4.1.2.3: all banking and/or deposit-taking entities that operate in a jurisdiction where compliance with the LCR is a regulatory requirement shall be included; §4.1.2.4: all banking and/or deposit-taking entities that operate in jurisdictions where the LCR is not a regulatory requirement shall be included in accordance with the provisions of regulation 36 of the Regulations.` Regulations Relating to Banks Reg 36 (consolidated supervision inclusion). | Calculate the consolidated LCR including only banking and/or deposit-taking entities; consolidate branches in accordance with §2.5 home/host jurisdiction parameters; include all banking entities in LCR-regulated jurisdictions; include those in non-LCR-regulated jurisdictions per Regulation 36. | Liquidity Risk Management Policy; LCR Policy; Consolidated Supervision Policy | Eitan (Treasurer, governance); Bea (Accounting & financial reporting engineer, engineering) — consolidated LCR calculation | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-RISK-LIQUIDITY, ACT-REPORT-PRUDENTIAL | riskTaxonomy: RT-LQ.FN, RT-LR.RC | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

In this regard, the HQLA of the relevant entities shall be capped at the lower of the specified minimum LCR requirement in each relevant jurisdiction or the home jurisdiction in the summation process. Any excess HQLA or inflows of a particular entity shall only be included in the consolidated LCR once it has been evaluated for transferability across jurisdictions and convertibility across currencies, and if it is freely available to the consolidated (parent) entity in times of stress.` | Sum net cash outflows and HQLA portfolios of all consolidated entities to calculate the consolidated LCR; cap entity-level HQLA at the lower of the minimum LCR requirement in the relevant jurisdiction or the home jurisdiction; include excess HQLA or inflows in the consolidated LCR only if evaluated as freely transferable across jurisdictions and convertible across currencies and available to the parent entity in stress. | Liquidity Risk Management Policy; LCR Policy; HQLA Management Policy | Eitan (Treasurer, governance) — HQLA transferability assessment; Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — consolidated LCR computation | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-RISK-LIQUIDITY | riskTaxonomy: RT-LQ.FN | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
