## Domain PR-NSFR — Net Stable Funding Ratio: Calibration and Implementation (D1/2023)

> *Added 2026-05-28 (v1.36) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) via structured-JSON extraction of `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d1-2023-structured.json`. D1/2023 is issued by the SARB Prudential Authority under Banks Act s.6(6)(a), effective 1 January 2023 (signed by Fundi Tshazibana, Chief Executive Officer). It replaces Directive 8 of 2017 (§1.4). The directive applies to all banks, branches of foreign institutions, and controlling companies. The NSFR framework became effective 1 January 2021 following publication of amended Regulations in Government Gazette No. 44003 on 18 December 2020 (§1.5). The directive provides calibration guidance on the NSFR per Regulation 26(14)(d): ASF factors, RSF factors, and treatment of specific items including the Restricted Committed Liquidity Facility (RCLF), derivative liabilities, interdependent assets and liabilities, and contingent funding obligations. **Key feature: ASF phase-out schedule (§2.2.5).** The PA deviates from international standards for secured/unsecured ZAR funding from financial corporate customers (excluding banks) with residual maturity < 6 months, phasing out the 35% SA-specific ASF factor as follows: 30% (1 June 2023 – 31 Dec 2023); 20% (1 Jan 2024 – 31 Dec 2024); 10% (1 Jan 2025 – 31 Dec 2027); 0% (from 1 Jan 2028). **Bind-trigger:** `COMMENCEMENT-BIND` (NSFR calibration obligations arise at commencement of trading; ASF phase-out schedule carries specific wall-clock dates). **Note on existing rows:** `ORG-PR-43` (v1.16 high-level D1/2023 NSFR placeholder per `ORG-PR-RETURNS-004` BA 120) remains; the new `ORG-PR-NSFR-001..012` rows are granular supplements extracted from D1/2023 verbatim directive text. All rows: `Entity scope: bank`, `review-status: new-from-llm-extraction`.*

| ID | URN | Citation | Requirement | Fulfilment policy | Owner | Status | Bind-trigger | Entity scope | Applies-at | Product scope | Activity scope | Risk taxonomy | review-status | review-author | review-date | review-event-id |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ORG-PR-NSFR-001 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d1-2023-nsfr-calculation-formula:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D1/2023** §2.1.2. `§2.1.2: The NSFR should be calculated in accordance with Regulation 26(14)(d) of the Regulations relating to Banks by dividing the available amount of stable funding by the required amount of stable funding and multiplying it by one hundred.` Regulations Relating to Banks Reg 26(14)(d). | Calculate the NSFR as: (Available Stable Funding / Required Stable Funding) × 100, in accordance with Regulation 26(14)(d) of the Regulations Relating to Banks. | Liquidity Risk Management Policy; NSFR Policy; `Procedures/by-policy/lcr-nsfr-liquidity-stress.md` (planned) | Eitan (Treasurer, governance) — NSFR calculation; Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — NSFR computation engine | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-RISK-LIQUIDITY | riskTaxonomy: RT-LQ.FN | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-NSFR-002 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d1-2023-nsfr-asf-long-term-stability:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D1/2023** §2.2.1–§2.2.3. `§2.2.1: The amount of ASF is measured based on the broad characteristics of the relative stability of an institution's funding sources, including the contractual maturity of its liabilities and the differences in the propensity of different types of funding providers to withdraw their funding. §2.2.2: The ASF's calibration presupposes that long-term funding is more stable than short-term funding. §2.2.3: The NSFR framework assumes that regulatory capital and funding provided by retail and small business customers are the most stable sources of funding and have the highest ASF factors assigned to them.` Regulations Relating to Banks Reg 26(14). | Measure the Available Stable Funding (ASF) based on the stability characteristics of funding sources, applying the ASF factor hierarchy: regulatory capital and retail/small business deposits carry the highest ASF factors; long-term funding receives higher ASF factors than short-term funding. | Liquidity Risk Management Policy; NSFR Policy; Capital Management Policy | Eitan (Treasurer, governance) — ASF measurement; Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — ASF calculation; Camille (Chief Financial Officer, finance) — regulatory capital classification | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-RISK-LIQUIDITY | riskTaxonomy: RT-LQ.FN | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-NSFR-003 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d1-2023-nsfr-asf-non-financial-corporate:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D1/2023** §2.2.4. `§2.2.4: Funding with a residual maturity of less than one year received from non-financial corporate customers, sovereigns and public-sector entities, as well as funding from financial corporate customers with a residual maturity of between six months and one year, shall be assigned an ASF factor of 50 per cent.` | Assign an ASF factor of 50% to: (a) funding with residual maturity of less than one year from non-financial corporate customers, sovereigns, and public-sector entities; and (b) funding from financial corporate customers with residual maturity between six months and one year. | Liquidity Risk Management Policy; NSFR Policy | Eitan (Treasurer, governance); Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — ASF factor application | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-RISK-LIQUIDITY | riskTaxonomy: RT-LQ.FN | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-PR-NSFR-004 | `urn:obligation:bank:prudential:pa-d1-2023-nsfr-asf-phase-out-zar-financial-corporate:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D1/2023** §2.2.5. `§2.2.5: The NSFR framework assigns an ASF factor of zero per cent to funding with a residual maturity of less than six months from financial corporate customers. The PA concluded in 2017 that it would be appropriate to deviate from the international standard and to assign an ASF factor of 35 per cent to secured and unsecured funding received in Rand (ZAR) from financial corporate customers, excluding banks, with a residual maturity of less than six months. This dispensation was only available for banks conducting business in South Africa. In order to be fully compliant with the NSFR framework, the PA has decided to phase out the ASF factor in this regard as follows: Table 1 Period ASF Percentage From 1 June 2023 to 31 December 2023 30 From 1 January 2024 to 31 December 2024 20 From 1 January 2025 to 31 December 2027 10 From 1 January 2028 onwards 0.` | Apply the phased ASF factor for secured and unsecured ZAR funding from financial corporate customers (excluding banks) with residual maturity < 6 months, according to the D1/2023 phase-out table: 30% (1 June 2023 – 31 Dec 2023); 20% (1 Jan 2024 – 31 Dec 2024); 10% (1 Jan 2025 – 31 Dec 2027); 0% (from 1 Jan 2028). The international standard of 0% applies to all other currencies and from 1 January 2028 onwards. | Liquidity Risk Management Policy; NSFR Policy | Eitan (Treasurer, governance) — ASF phase-out monitoring; Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — date-dependent ASF factor computation | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-RISK-LIQUIDITY | riskTaxonomy: RT-LQ.FN | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

Accordingly, the PA's assessment that the RSF factors set out in the NSFR framework is appropriate and shall be implemented accordingly by banks.` Regulations Relating to Banks Reg 26(14). | Measure Required Stable Funding (RSF) based on the liquidity risk profile of assets and off-balance sheet exposures; implement the RSF factors set out in the BCBS NSFR framework as calibrated for South Africa by the PA, taking into account resilient credit extension, bank behaviour, liquidity value, asset tenor, and asset quality. | Liquidity Risk Management Policy; NSFR Policy; Asset and Liability Management Policy | Eitan (Treasurer, governance); Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — RSF calculation; Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — off-balance sheet exposure identification | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-RISK-LIQUIDITY | riskTaxonomy: RT-LQ.FN | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

Any excess cash reserve balances held at the South African Reserve Bank in the relevant reserve account shall be assigned an RSF factor of 0 per cent.` Regulations Relating to Banks Reg 26(14). | Assign an RSF factor of 5% to the bank's minimum required cash reserves; assign an RSF factor of 0% to any excess cash reserve balances held at the South African Reserve Bank in the relevant reserve account. | Liquidity Risk Management Policy; NSFR Policy | Eitan (Treasurer, governance); Rohan (Market Risk Engineer, risk engineering) — SARB reserve account RSF classification | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | entity-only | universal | ACT-RISK-LIQUIDITY | riskTaxonomy: RT-LQ.FN | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

Interdependent assets and liabilities must be approved at the very least by the executive committee of the relevant bank responsible for liquidity risk management monitoring.` BCBS NSFR framework §45. | To benefit from the NSFR beneficial treatment for interdependent assets and liabilities: obtain supervisory approval from the Prudential Authority; ensure careful evaluation of internal governance mechanisms; and obtain approval from at minimum the bank's executive committee responsible for liquidity risk management monitoring. | Liquidity Risk Management Policy; NSFR Policy; Governance and Internal Controls Policy | Eitan (Treasurer, governance) — supervisory approval request; Devon (Chief Operating Officer, governance) — executive committee approval; Helena (Chief Risk Officer, governance) — governance evaluation | NOT_APPLICABLE | commencement | bank | entity-only | universal | ACT-RISK-LIQUIDITY, ACT-GOVERN-COMPLIANCE | riskTaxonomy: RT-LQ.FN | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

## Domain GV-FNP — Fit-and-Proper: Beneficial Owner Criminal Background Checks (D6/2022)

> *Added 2026-05-29 (v1.37) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) via structured-JSON extraction of `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d6-2022-structured.json`. D6/2022 is issued by the SARB Prudential Authority under Banks Act s.6(6)(a), signed 12 August 2022 by Fundi Tshazibana (Chief Executive Officer, PA). It applies to all banks, branches of foreign institutions, and controlling companies registered or operating in South Africa. The directive implements FATF Recommendation 26 and BCBS Core Principles for Effective Banking Supervision (Essential Criteria 5, Principle 5) in respect of beneficial owner (BO) fit-and-proper screening. Banks Act s.39 (furnishing of information by shareholders), s.51 (application of Companies Act to banks and controlling companies), s.52 (subsidiaries, branch offices), s.59 (returns regarding shareholders), s.67 (disclosure of certain shareholders), and Reg 36(17)(b)(ii) (FATF Recommendation 26 implementation) are the legislative anchors. **Bind-trigger:** `LICENCE-BIND` (beneficial-owner CBCR submissions are required at the bank-establishment application stage under Banks Act s.12 and bind on an ongoing basis from licence-day for all existing BOs; periodic re-screening binds post-licence). **Note on existing rows:** no prior granular D6/2022 rows exist in the register. The new `ORG-GV-FNP-001..005` rows are the first granular extraction of D6/2022. All rows: `Entity scope: bank`, `review-status: new-from-llm-extraction`.*

| ID | URN | Citation | Requirement | Fulfilment policy | Owner | Status | Bind-trigger | Entity scope | Applies-at | Product scope | Activity scope | Risk taxonomy | review-status | review-author | review-date | review-event-id |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ORG-GV-FNP-001 | `urn:obligation:bank:gv:pa-d6-2022-fnp-bo-ownership-record:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D6/2022** §1.1.2 + §2.1–§2.2. `§1.1.2: Banks are ultimately responsible for ensuring that they understand and keep record of their ownership structures, including BOs. §2.1: This directive applies to all banks, branches of foreign institutions and controlling companies registered or operating in South Africa in terms of the Banks Act, 1990 (Act No. 94 of 1990). §2.2: Institutions are required to ensure compliance with the requirements set out herein.` Enabling authority: Banks Act 94/1990 s.6(6)(a); Banks Act s.39 (furnishing of information by shareholders). | Maintain accurate and up-to-date records of the bank's ownership structure, including the identity of all beneficial owners (BOs), and provide an organogram to the Prudential Authority depicting the ownership structure and the assignment of percentages explaining how BOs were identified. | Governance Framework — Beneficial Owner Register; Fit-and-Proper Policy; `Procedures/by-policy/directors-executive-officers-fit-and-proper.md` (planned) | Owen (Company Secretary, governance) — BO register curatorship; Iris (Regulator-relations engineer, engineering) — PA submission channel | NOT_APPLICABLE | licence-day | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-GOVERN-BOARD, ACT-CORP-ENTITY | riskTaxonomy: RT-ST.GV | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-GV-FNP-002 | `urn:obligation:bank:gv:pa-d6-2022-fnp-bo-cbcr-on-request:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D6/2022** §1.4.1 (Directive §2.2.1–§2.2.2). `Directive 2.2.1: Banks must, at the written request of the PA, submit criminal background check reports (CBCRs) to the PA for each of their BOs. 2.2.2: Banks must ensure that they examine all the levels in their ownership structure and provide the PA with an organogram depicting the said structure and the assignment of percentages, explaining how the BOs were identified.` Banks Act 94/1990 s.6(6)(a); Reg 36(17)(b)(ii) (FATF Recommendation 26). | Submit criminal background check reports (CBCRs) — or police clearance certificates — to the Prudential Authority for each beneficial owner upon the PA's written request, together with an ownership-structure organogram detailing how BOs were identified at each level of the structure. | Fit-and-Proper Policy; Governance Framework — Beneficial Owner Register; `Procedures/by-policy/directors-executive-officers-fit-and-proper.md` (planned) | Owen (Company Secretary, governance) — BO CBCR coordination; Iris (Regulator-relations engineer, engineering) — PA written-request tracking and submission | NOT_APPLICABLE | licence-day | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-GOVERN-BOARD, ACT-CORP-ENTITY | riskTaxonomy: RT-ST.GV | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-GV-FNP-003 | `urn:obligation:bank:gv:pa-d6-2022-fnp-bo-cbcr-freshness:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D6/2022** §1.4.1 (Directive §2.2.5–§2.2.6 + §2.2.7). `2.2.5: CBCRs in respect of domestic BOs (South African nationals) must not be older than 30 calendar days from the date of submitting the information to the PA. 2.2.6: CBCRs or an equivalent document in respect of BOs identified as foreign nationals must not be older than 60 calendar days from the date of submitting the information to the PA. 2.2.7: CBCRs for domestic BOs can be requested or sourced from CBC service providers nationwide. These CBCRs will contain a unique CBCR reference number. Banks must ensure that this unique CBCR reference number is clearly visible when submitting the document to the PA.` | Ensure that CBCRs submitted for South African national BOs are no older than 30 calendar days from the submission date; CBCRs for foreign national BOs are no older than 60 calendar days from the submission date; and that each CBCR carries the unique CBCR reference number issued by the CBC service provider. | Fit-and-Proper Policy; Governance Framework — Beneficial Owner Register | Owen (Company Secretary, governance) — CBCR freshness tracking; Iris (Regulator-relations engineer, engineering) — submission-date recording | NOT_APPLICABLE | licence-day | bank | entity-only | universal | ACT-GOVERN-BOARD, ACT-CORP-ENTITY | riskTaxonomy: RT-ST.GV | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-GV-FNP-004 | `urn:obligation:bank:gv:pa-d6-2022-fnp-bo-change-notification:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D6/2022** §1.4.1 (Directive §2.3.1). `2.3.1: Banks are required to: submit updated CBCRs to the PA within the timelines outlined in paragraphs 2.2.5 and 2.2.6 upon becoming aware of any changes to their BO(s) structure; and maintain up-to-date and accurate information pertaining to the identity of their BO(s).` Banks Act s.39, s.67 (disclosure of certain shareholders). | Submit updated CBCRs to the Prudential Authority within the applicable freshness timelines (30 days for SA nationals; 60 days for foreign nationals) upon becoming aware of any changes to the beneficial owner structure; and maintain up-to-date and accurate beneficial owner identity information at all times. | Fit-and-Proper Policy; Governance Framework — Beneficial Owner Register; Regulatory Change Management Policy | Owen (Company Secretary, governance) — BO structure change monitoring; Iris (Regulator-relations engineer, engineering) — PA notification submission | NOT_APPLICABLE | licence-day | bank | consolidated | universal | ACT-GOVERN-BOARD, ACT-CORP-ENTITY | riskTaxonomy: RT-ST.GV | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |
| ORG-GV-FNP-005 | `urn:obligation:bank:gv:pa-d6-2022-fnp-bo-periodic-rescreening:v1` | SARB Prudential Authority **Directive D6/2022** §1.4.1 (Directive §2.4.1). `2.4.1: The PA will require banks to submit CBCRs periodically and in line with the PA's application of a risk-based approach to supervision. Banks must re-screen their BOs on a periodic basis to ensure that the information they hold is up to date and accurate. The CBCRs should be provided to the PA within 30 calendar days of completing such screenings.` | Re-screen all beneficial owners on a periodic basis consistent with the PA's risk-based supervisory approach; provide the resulting CBCRs to the Prudential Authority within 30 calendar days of completing each re-screening exercise. | Fit-and-Proper Policy; Governance Framework — Beneficial Owner Register; Regulatory Engagement Policy | Owen (Company Secretary, governance) — periodic re-screening schedule; Iris (Regulator-relations engineer, engineering) — PA CBCR submission within 30 days of screening | NOT_APPLICABLE | licence-day | bank | entity-only | universal | ACT-GOVERN-BOARD, ACT-CORP-ENTITY | riskTaxonomy: RT-ST.GV | new-from-llm-extraction |  |  |  |

## Domain GV-FNP-S — Fit-and-Proper (Supplementary): Directors and Executive Officers CBCR Requirements (D7/2022)

> *Added 2026-05-29 (v1.37) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) via structured-JSON extraction of `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d7-2022-structured.json`. D7/2022 is issued by the SARB Prudential Authority under Banks Act s.6(6)(a), signed 12 August 2022 by Fundi Tshazibana (Chief Executive Officer, PA). It applies to all banks, branches of foreign institutions, and controlling companies registered or operating in South Africa. D7/2022 is the supplementary directive on fit-and-proper criminal background check requirements for directors and executive officers; it supplements the existing Reg 42 + form BA 020 regime (Banks Act s.60(5)(a)) with mandatory CBCR requirements effective from 1 September 2022. D7/2022 also applies to cross-border banking operations under Banks Act ss.52(1)(a) and 52(1)(b). **Note on existing rows:** `ORG-PR-42` (v1.27 high-level D7/2022 CBC directors-and-executives obligation) remains as a legacy-unreviewed high-level row. The new `ORG-GV-FNP-S-001..006` rows are granular supplements extracted from D7/2022 §2.1.1–§2.1.13 verbatim text, providing obligation-level granularity beyond the high-level ORG-PR-42 summary. **Bind-trigger:** `LICENCE-BIND` (CBCRs for all prospective directors and executive officers are required at the bank-establishment application stage under Banks Act s.12 per D7/2022 §2.1.10; ongoing re-screening binds from licence-day). All rows: `Entity scope: bank`, `review-status: new-from-llm-extraction`.*

| ID | URN | Citation | Requirement | Fulfilment policy | Owner | Status | Bind-trigger | Entity scope | Applies-at | Product scope | Activity scope | Risk taxonomy | review-status | review-author | review-date | review-event-id |

## Domain PR-REP43 — Auditor Reporting Requirements under Regulation 46 (D2/2024)

> *Added 2026-05-29 (v1.37) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) via structured-JSON extraction of `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d2-2024-structured.json`. D2/2024 is issued by the SARB Prudential Authority under Banks Act s.6(6)(a), signed 08 March 2024 by Fundi Tshazibana (Chief Executive Officer, PA). It applies to all banks, branches of foreign institutions, and controlling companies registered or operating in South Africa. The directive directs auditors of banks to perform engagements per the Annexure 1 audit matrix for financial years ended on or after 30 September 2023, and to render their reports in accordance with the IRBA illustrative regulatory reports (Parts A to E for SA operations; Parts A to D for foreign operations). Regulation 46(1) of the Regulations Relating to Banks requires auditors to report annually within 120 days of financial year end on the bank's financial position and operations. **Bind-trigger:** `COMMENCEMENT-BIND` (auditor reports on BA returns arise once the bank is trading and has a financial year end to report on; the first Reg 46 audit report obligation is triggered by the bank's first financial year end post-commencement of trading). **Note on existing rows:** `ORG-PR-29` (v1.14 high-level D2/2024 placeholder, capturing the Reg 46 BA-return reporting obligation) remains. The new `ORG-PR-REP43-001..003` rows are granular supplements extracted from D2/2024 §1–§2 verbatim text, focussing on the auditor engagement requirements specifically mandated by the directive. All rows: `Entity scope: bank`, `review-status: new-from-llm-extraction`.*

## Domain PR-RETURNS2 — Returns to be Submitted to the Prudential Authority (D6/2025 Supplement)

> *Added 2026-05-29 (v1.37) by Mira (Compliance / RegTech engineer, engineering — reports to Zara (Chief Compliance Officer, governance)) via structured-JSON extraction of `Regulations/Banks/source-docs/banks-d6-2025-structured.json`. D6/2025 is issued by the SARB Prudential Authority under Banks Act s.6(6)(a), effective 1 July 2025. It applies to all banks, branches of foreign institutions, and controlling companies registered or operating in South Africa. The directive sets out the returns to be submitted to the PA with effect from 1 July 2025, and remains effective as confirmed in Banks Act Circular 1 of the relevant year (§1.3). **Note on existing rows:** `ORG-PR-RETURNS-001..028` (v1.35 granular D5/2025 BA returns obligations) and the general Reg 46 framework rows (`ORG-PR-29`) remain. The new `ORG-PR-RETURNS2-001..002` rows capture D6/2025 as a supplementary returns directive effective from 1 July 2025, distinct from D5/2025. **Bind-trigger:** `COMMENCEMENT-BIND` (returns obligations under D6/2025 arise at commencement of trading; the specific effective date of 1 July 2025 is a wall-clock trigger for the returns regime). All rows: `Entity scope: bank`, `review-status: new-from-llm-extraction`.*
